#2911. Dubois vs Han: Lone Star Showdown in Texas Set February 20

Undefeated world champions Caroline Dubois and Stephanie Han came face to face for the first time ahead of their announced unification fight.

Dubois vs Han will appear in the main event of MVPW09 scheduled on Saturday, February 20 at GECU Arena in El Paso.

At stake are the WBO, WBC, and WBA world lightweight titles – and a fight with the winner of Turhan vs Baumgardner.

Ahead of Sunday's blockbuster MVPW07 fight card, undefeated world champions Caroline Dubois of London (14-0-1, 5 KOs) and Stephanie Han of El Paso, Texas (13-0, 3 KOs) engaged in their first faceoff ahead of their unification fight for three lightweight world titles in the main event of MVPW09.

The card will take place on Saturday, February 20, 2027, at GECU Arena at the Don Haskins Center in El Paso, Texas. Dubois vs Han will headline a full card of championship fights in the biggest fight for these two women.

Fans can sign up for presale for MVPW09: Dubois vs. Han HERE. Public on-sale begins Thursday, December 3, at 10 a.m. MT on Ticketmaster.com

The two champions came to Arlington, Texas, for their first face-to-face meeting as special guests at fight week of MVPW07: Turhan vs. Baumgardner taking place on Sunday on ESPN and ESPN Deportes from College Park Center.

"This fight definitely makes sense," said Han at Friday's news conference. "Caroline's ranked number one in my division. She has two belts. She's an Olympian, and I'm just excited for this opportunity. And I'm excited to have this in El Paso, Texas."

"This is just another step on the road for me," said Dubois. "I can't wait. Understood, baby. Let's fucking go!"

Both Dubois and Han have a vested interest in Sunday's main event between IBF World Lightweight champion Elif Nur Turhan and challenger Alycia Baumgardner of the United States. The winners of both bouts are expected to have a showdown later in 2027 to become the undisputed lightweight champion.

"I love it. I've been dreaming of moments like this," said Dubois. "Alycia's been running and ducking and diving for as long as she can. She's made excuses, but now we're ahead. We're close. I have to get through Han. She has to get through Turhan, and we're gonna make it happen."

Han doesn't plan to make it easy. "I definitely am the underdog, but I'm a champion for a reason. No one is going to get through me. I'm going to stay champion, and I am the future undisputed lightweight champion," insisted Han.

Caroline Dubois staked her claim to the division with a win over former champion Terri Harper. Photo: Most Valuable Promotions

Dubois, the southpaw known as "Sweet Caroline," enjoyed a decorated amateur career that included gold at the 2018 Youth Olympic Games, four European Youth Championships and representing Great Britain at the Tokyo Olympics.

Dubois has been a pro since 2022. She won the WBC world title before unifying the WBC and WBO championships with a victory over Terri Harper at MVPW01 in April 2026. The younger sister of heavyweight world champion Daniel Dubois, Caroline Dubois recently defended her titles against Amelia Moore in August.

"I can't wait to get back in the ring, and I can't wait to go into Stephanie Han's hometown and add another world title to my collection. I respect what she's accomplished, but I'm coming to El Paso to take her title and show everyone why I'm the best lightweight in the world.

"These are the kinds of fights I've been working toward my entire career. I want to prove myself against the best. On Saturday, February 20, I'm coming to make a statement and take another step toward becoming undisputed. A big thank you to MVP and Sky for making this fight happen and continuing to give women's boxing the platform it deserves."

Stephanie Han held off a surging Holly Holm to retain her WBA World Lightweight title in their rematch in May. It's boxing. Photo: Copper Nell / Most Valuable Promotions

Han, an El Paso native, will have the hometown advantage. She is a mother of two and a veteran of the El Paso Police Department. Known as "The Enforcer," Han captured the vacant WBA lightweight world title with a first-round knockout of Hannah Terlep in February 2025.

Han has defended her title three times, including back-to-back victories over boxing legend Holly Holm, most recently on May 30. She has balanced boxing, her law enforcement career, and motherhood until signing with MVP and finally getting the chance to devote herself to boxing full time.

Han called the showdown "a dream come true. Finally, I have the opportunity to unify titles in my division and get one step closer to becoming the undisputed queen of the lightweight division.

Expect the winner on Sunday night to declare her intentions toward both Dubois and Han, and mark your calendar for February 20.

"To be able to fight at home at GECU Arena at the Don Haskins Center is incredibly special, and I'm grateful to MVP for their support to make this happen," said Han, encouraging all her hometown fans to show up.

Nakisa Bidarian, CEO of Most Valuable Promotions, said in a statement announcing the matchup, "Caroline Dubois and Stephanie Han are two undefeated world champions at the top of one of the deepest and most competitive divisions in boxing.

"Stephanie's story is remarkable. She's a police officer, a mother, a former beauty queen and a world champion who has become a source of pride for El Paso.

"Caroline has established herself as one of the most talented young champions in the sport, and bringing these two women together with three major world titles on the line is a huge moment for MVP and women's boxing."

#2909. 49ers-Raiders predictions: All eyes on Nick Bosa and DeForest Buckner

The San Francisco 49ers are coming off their bye week with some added reinforcements, as they look to start the second half of the season on the right track against the Las Vegas Raiders this weekend.

The Niners are 9.5-point favorites on FanDuel Sportsbook, with the game total set at 51.5.

Stay connected to the action and share every reaction with fellow 49ers fans on the SB Nation app.

Download the SB Nation app on iOS or Android.

While the 49ers will not have wide receivers Mike Evans, Deebo Samuel, or Christian Kirk, Demarcus Robinson is trending toward playing, while cornerbacks Upton Stout and Nate Hobbs are looking in that direction as well.

Additionally, San Francisco will have Nick Bosa back, so he and DeForest Buckner should be on the field on Sunday as the 49ers debut their new-look defensive line.

Heading into the weekend, the 49ers are massive 10-point favorites over the Raiders after opening as 8.5-point favorites, with the total skyrocketing to 51.5 points.

While the 49ers are tied for the league's best record at 6-1, the Raiders have been on a bit of a slide after their hot 3-0 start. They suffered four straight losses to the Kansas City Chiefs, New England Patriots, Buffalo Bills, and Los Angeles Rams before their come-from-behind win over the New York Jets last weekend.

So, let's look at our three predictions for Sunday's game, which is set to kick off at 1:25 p.m. on FOX.

Demarcus Robinson returns with a big play

The 49ers have missed that vertical field-stretcher since Demarcus Robinson went out weeks ago. Initially, that was set to be De'Zhaun Stribling, but he suffered the ankle injury back in Week 1. So, that went to Demarcus Robinson, who played in just a game and a half before suffering a high ankle sprain.

Robinson has been out since Week 2, but he was involved in both of the games he played to start the year. He had the long 39-yard touchdown against the Rams in Week 1, finishing with two catches for 50 yards.

Then, in Week 2 against the Miami Dolphins, he had three catches for 34 yards, with the longest going for 19.

The 49ers wide receiver core is still beaten up, and the group has struggled with separation at times. That's where Robinson comes in, and it feels like Kyle Shanahan will look his way for a couple of vertical plays. I think one hits on Sunday.

Nick Bosa, DeForest Buckner return with a sack

Kirk Cousins has taken 11 sacks this season, as Klint Kubiak and the Raiders have done a great job getting the ball out of his hands. Cousins has averaged a 2.70-second time to throw, and he's been even quicker against the blitz.

All 11 of his sacks have come when teams have rushed four or fewer, and I think the 49ers will find their way to at least one courtesy of returners Nick Bosa and DeForest Buckner this weekend.

Las Vegas's run game has not been great. That should leave the Raiders in some tough late-down situations, and the 49ers suddenly have a pretty impressive veteran pass-rushing group with Bosa, Buckner, and Osa Odighizuwa.

Now, others might get home for even more sacks, but I think the stars are aligning for Bosa or Buckner to get to the quarterback at least once on Sunday.

One way the Raiders' passing game has been thriving this season is by throwing to Ashton Jeanty, one of the most targeted running backs in the NFL this season.

Now, Jeanty may have gotten a soft benching last week, as his snap share dipped significantly in favor of rookie Mike Washington, but he's still a very talented pass catcher, and that leaves Fred Warner with the top assignment this weekend.

Not only will Warner likely carry star Brock Bowers for a few routes, but he'll also work in coverage against the running backs, the two spots Las Vegas has looked to target.

Jeanty is second on the Raiders with 37 receptions for 376 yards and three touchdowns. The 49ers will have to mitigate that on Sunday.

#2907. Report: Judge's ruling could make Bellarmine eligible for CCS football playoffs

Bellarmine's football playoff season is still alive. At least temporarily.

According to the San Jose Mercury News, a Santa Clara County judge issued a temporary restraining order against the Central Coast Section, which on Monday ruled the Bells would have to forfeit their six wins this season for using an ineligible player.

The CCS ruling could not be appealed according to section officials, so Bellarmine, as principal Rod Jemison promised, went to court. Superior Court Judge Panteha E. Saban rendered the TRO on Friday, with another hearing scheduled for Thursday, the Mercury News reported.

The problem with that is that the CCS starts its playoffs next week and plans to announce those pairings are scheduled for Sunday.

Bellarmine (6-3, 3-3 West Catholic Athletic League) closes its regular season Saturday at Riordan. Using a point system and MaxPreps computer rankings, the Bells are likely to be seeded in the bottom half of the CCS Division 1 bracket, the most competitive of the five divisions.

If the forfeits stand, the Bells will be 0-9 and couldn't qualify for the CCS playoffs, even with a win at Riordan. But because of the judge's order, Bellarmine would likely be back in unless the CCS delays its pairings to Thursday, which is highly unlikely.

Messages to CCS Commissioner Dave Grissom were not immediately answered. Nor were they earlier in the week when the announcement of Bellarmine's forfeits was made.

In a letter to parents attained by the Chronicle this week, Jemison wrote: "We do not dispute the violation occurred. However, we disagree with the severity of the associated consequences and their impact on the entire team.

"Bellarmine received notice of a violation of CIF Central Coast Section (CCS) by-laws regarding athletic eligibility for transfer students. The CCS Commissioner outlined consequences for the violation, including athletic ineligibility for the student-athlete in question and retroactive forfeiture of games in which that student participated.

"We acknowledge it is Bellarmine's responsibility to ensure compliance and understanding of CIF and CCS regulations, and we are committed to identifying and addressing any gaps in our oversight."

A judge reportedly agreed that the punishment might have been too great and wants to discuss the issues.

If the CCS seeds Bellarmine into the Division 1 playoffs, then the court rules the forfeits were justisfied, what the section will do then will be of note. One possibility is that the team scheduled to play the Bells would receive a first-round bye.

#2905. INTERVIEW: Makaay not concerned with Bayern's striker depth, holds squad among Champions League favourites

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews) Roy Makaay is not concerned about the lack of depth at striker in Vincent Kompany's squad. The former FC Bayern marksman believes several players can cover for Harry Kane in the event of an emergency.

“I haven’t spoken with Harry [Kane] about it, but I don’t think he likes to be on the bench that often. Kompany has given him some rest [here and there] which is perfect," Makaay told Bulinews.com and other media at a round table event.

“If the game [result] is clear, you can almost give him the day off. I think [the lack of depth at striker] is why they signed [Ismael] Saibari as well. I know Saibari from the Dutch league, he was really amazing last season.

“He can play several positions. Of course, he prefers to play the number 10 role, but for the Moroccan national team he is the striker. I think [Serge] Gnabry has played as a striker for the [German] national team as well.

“Last year, they had solutions like [Nicolas] Jackson, this year that’s why they bought Saibari because he can cover a lot [of positions]. Luis Diaz was maybe the only one on the left wing, even if Phonzie Davies can play there.

“Last year, he [Davies] was injured as well, so with the signings of [Nathaniel] Brown and Saibari there are more possibilities to replace Harry Kane if he needs a rest,” the 51-year-old double Bundesliga winner explained.

Delving into the UEFA Champions League and Bayern's chances of picking up the title for the first time since 2020, Makaay believes the German record champions are right in the mix but may need some good fortune in the knockout draw.

“They are one of the top teams like Paris [Saint-Germain]. Like last season, it’s the most difficult [competition] to win because you have 5 or 6 teams who could win it,” said Makaay, who was unlucky not to lift the trophy in his playing career.

“It depends a little bit on the draw because all the big teams were on one side like Man City, Real Madrid, Liverpool, Bayern and PSG. Of course, the first goal for Bayern is making the top 8 to avoid the extra [qualifying] game.

“Sure, you need a little bit of luck in the draw [for the knockouts] as well, but I can see them winning it. Of course, PSG, Real Madrid, Barcelona are playing really well, but [Bayern] have the team to win it,” he concluded.

#2904. Brandon Aiyuk keeps motive close to vest in statement outside 49ers office

SANTA CLARA — A large, black Mercedes van pulled up outside the 49ers' team offices, a stone's throw from Levi's Stadium, the side door swung open and out stepped Brandon Aiyuk.

The erstwhile wide receiver ended his longstanding refusal to step foot on the team's property Friday afternoon, appearing just before 3 p.m. for a meeting with front office officials.

Both sides kept their cards close to the vest, but the summit represented the first step in Aiyuk resuming his NFL career that has been on pause since injuring his knee in Week 7 of the 2024 season.

San Francisco 49ers wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk warms up before an NFL football game against the Arizona Cardinals in Santa Clara, Calif., Oct. 6, 2024. AP Photo/Jed Jacobsohn

The 49ers made no acknowledgment of the meeting besides coach Kyle Shanahan, who was asked about it Friday and said he did not plan on being involved in any way.

"It's been over for our football team for a long time," Shanahan said. "So I don't hear much, except questions about him today."

A little more than an hour after they arrived, Aiyuk and Drew Rosenhaus emerged from the office building with "nothing to report," according to the agent, who only began representing Aiyuk this week.

Aiyuk responded to one question from the California Post: Who was in the meeting?

"I met with the bosses, man," he said. "Big bosses. Big business."

There remained no immediate resolution to Aiyuk's situation. Currently going unpaid on the 49ers' reserve/left squad list, the 28-year-old receiver must first apply for reinstatement to the NFL.

In a brief statement upon arriving at the team offices, Aiyuk said that was the goal.

"Nice to see y'all," Aiyuk said, greeting the horde of about a dozen reporters and cameramen, wearing a matching Adidas camouflage hat and track jacket with dark blue pants.

"I'm feeling great, feeling healthy. Long recovery process, long rehab process. But I'm back to 100% and looking to get reinstated into the National Football League."

The stakeout spectacle was put into motion when ESPN insider Adam Schefter broke the news Monday that Aiyuk had hired Rosenhaus, a longtime and respected NFL agent, with the intent to get back to football.

The drama kicked into high gear Friday morning, when Schefter reported that Aiyuk and his new agent were en route to the 49ers' offices for a 3 p.m. meeting with team officials.

Shanahan's weekly Friday news conference that typically lasts 10-15 minutes was over in three minutes as reporters rushed to get eyes on the receiver who has been persona non grata around these parts for the better part of the past year and a half.

A television news chopper hovered overhead, tracking the van carrying Aiyuk and Rosenhaus. At one point, the van pulled up to the 49ers' team offices about an hour prior to the meeting, spotted four reporters already staking out the facility and turned around.

An hour later, the same van reappeared, and Aiyuk and Rosenhaus emerged.

"Obviously Brandon is here to meet with the team. Fortunately he's feeling good physically. He's healthy. He's ready to get back to work," Rosenhaus said in a prepared statement. "Don't really have any comment on where he's going to play. We'll keep that between us and the Niners. But obviously we're here with all the right intentions."

It will take more than the right intentions for Aiyuk to resume his NFL career, even if it's a step in the right direction. Despite signing a $120 million extension before the 2024 season, Aiyuk hasn't showed up to the team facilities since last fall and hasn't appeared in a game since that October.

Shanahan has repeatedly dismissed the idea of Aiyuk ever playing another snap for the 49ers, and again Friday afternoon, about two hours before Aiyuk's meeting with team brass, Shanahan said, he "could[n't] care less" about the outcome and that "your guess is as good as mine."

San Francisco 49ers wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk stands on the sideline before a game against the Los Angeles Rams in Inglewood, Calif., Sunday, Sept. 22, 2024. Getty Images

Then there is the matter of Aiyuk's legal trouble. There remains an outstanding warrant for his arrest in Santa Clara County, stemming from a video he posted to social media showing himself driving in excess of 100 mph down the road past Levi's Stadium.

Asked by The Post if Aiyuk had settled matters with police, Rosenhaus responded, "We're working on that."

Aiyuk also must come to an agreement with the 49ers on the $23 million bonus he received when he signed his extension in 2024. According to multiple reports Friday, San Francisco is demanding that he pay back $13 million of it before reinstating him.

The 49ers voided the remaining guaranteed money in Aiyuk's contract, citing his absence from the team facility while rehabbing the knee injury he suffered the last time he took the field for the 49ers.

Aiyuk tore his ACL and MCL seven weeks into his fifth NFL season. Since being drafted 25th overall out of Arizona State in 2020, Aiyuk had totaled 4,305 receiving yards and hauled in 25 touchdowns on 294 career receptions.

But he hasn't caught a pass — or stepped onto an NFL field — in more than two years.

San Francisco 49ers wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk (11) runs against the Los Angeles Rams in the first half of an NFL football game in Santa Clara, Calif., Oct. 18, 2020. AP

On Friday, he took the first step to getting back on the field.

It just isn't likely to happen in a 49ers uniform.

#2903. Kenny Atkinson isn’t concerned about Donovan Mitchell’s scoring numbers

CLEVELAND — Donovan Mitchell is taking four fewer shots per game this season compared to last year. Should the Cleveland Cavaliers be concerned?

"The shot attempt stuff doesn't bother me in the least," head coach Kenny Atkinson said when asked that very question.

Even though Mitchell is taking fewer shots, he's taking a similar percentage of looks at the rim. A quarter of Mitchell's attempts have been in the restricted area this season; that's slightly better than last season when he was taking 24% of his shots at the rim.

If you wanted to point at something more negative, you could say that he's shooting a lower percentage on shots at the rim and below the free-throw line. Additionally, his free-throw rate is also down slightly, and he's gone from driving 18.5 times per game to just 15.6.

That said, Mitchell has been slightly more efficient as he's averaging 1.24 points per shot attempt (88th percentile) and has an effective field-goal percentage of 57.4% (85th percentile).

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the Spida shirt HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cavs gear HERE.

Instead of worrying about Mitchell's shots, Atkinson is looking at something different.

"That's my theme to him: Can you make others better?" Atkinson said. "That's important for this group. You have to continue to make others better, make the right play. So I judge it more…I don't look at field goal attempts."

The blueprint for what Atkinson wants to see from Mitchell is last season's Game 7 against the Detroit Pistons. In that game, Mitchell came out with an effort to set up teammates, which led to him finishing with eight assists and no turnovers.

We've seen glimpses of this during the season. The first game against the Oklahoma City Thunder (when Mitchell had 11 assists) and the win over the Washington Wizards are both examples of that. He made a concerted effort to get others involved, and the Cavs were better off for it.

On the flip side, there's a balance to this. Mitchell's efforts to set up others need to come from being aggressive and getting to his spots on the floor. And from those spots, he needs to be getting others involved. That isn't something he is necessarily doing well early on. Mitchell sometimes feels a little too passive from an energy standpoint, which is where some of the criticism is coming from.

Ultimately, Mitchell is a scorer. But scoring will become easier for him when he's a threat to find teammates on his drives to the paint.

The Cavs are a work in progress at this point of the season, and so is their best player. The team is asking him to emphasize different skills than they have in previous years. And if he's able to do so consistently, they'll be better off for it in the long term.

"I know he can get 40 every night," Atkinson said. "I know he can shoot 25 times a game. He needs to empower others around him, and I think he does a hell of a job at it."

#2899. Inside the Rams’ secret shadow boxing league that is bringing the team together

Referees, ranked contenders, a leaderboard, and even a commissioner are overseeing the entire operation.

Welcome to the Los Angeles Rams shadow boxing league, where the competition is fierce, the trash talk is plentiful and the bragging rights might be worth more than the championship itself.

Oh, and at the center of it all is the reigning NFL Defensive Player of the Year, who also happens to be the undisputed champion of the league.

Instead of retreating into their phones between meetings, players are challenging teammates, laughing, talking trash and building relationships that extend beyond their position groups. Getty Images

What initially started as a harmless way to kill some time between meetings has blossomed into a full-fledged locker room phenomenon, bringing players together during a season filled with injuries, adversity and enormous expectations.

Rams linebacker Grant Stuard is the self-appointed commissioner, who has turned a childhood-style game into something resembling the UFC.

"We thought it'd be cool to get some organization going and get a league started," Stuard told the California Post in an exclusive interview Thursday.

Stuard and teammate Matthew Caldwell helped establish a running leaderboard that now features a top 10, position-group rankings and honorable mentions. Even injured players have found their way onto the board.

What initially started as a harmless way to kill some time between meetings has blossomed into a full-fledged locker room phenomenon. Getty Images

But at this point you're probably asking yourself the same question we did:

Despite its name, nobody is throwing any actual punches.

Two players stand face-to-face. One points up, down, left or right while the other simultaneously moves his head in a direction, hoping to avoid looking where his opponent points.

If the movements match, the attacker scores. Players alternate turns, attempting to anticipate each other's reactions and string together successful moves.

It's part reflexes, part mind games and entirely addictive. Throughout the season, easily over half the Rams locker room has participated. And because NFL players apparently can't compete at anything without turning it into its own organized sport, Stuard has established official rules.

Wide receiver Davante Adams, left, celebrates his touchdown with Puka Nacua. AP Photo/Scott Strazzante

Ranked matches are best-of-three and require a witness or referee. Matchups involving the top three competitors require two officials.

"If it's a top-three ranked match, you need two refs," Stuard explained. "You have to have some standards of what's acceptable play."

The rankings operate similar to the UFC's weight class rankings. An unranked player can challenge a ranked contender, and if the higher-ranked player accepts and loses, the leaderboard gets reshuffled.

Throughout the past several weeks rankings 2-10 have been erased more times than a toddler's Etch A Sketch, but one name has remained untouchable at the top of the leaderboard.

The best defensive player in the NFL today is also the reigning, defending, and undisputed king of the Rams shadow boxing league.

If and when the tournament takes place, Garrett's championship crown could finally be on the line. Abigail Dean /Los Angeles Rams

We've watched Garrett compete several times inside the locker room, and the results have been downright embarrassing for his challengers. Every matchup ends with Garrett victorious.

His reaction time is ridiculous, his anticipation uncanny and his ability to read opponents almost unfair.

"He has a great combination of reactive defense, but also very accurate offense," Stuard said. "He picks up on mannerisms and patterns."

Rams linebacker Shaun Dolac, who recently challenged Garrett himself and lost, agreed.

The Los Angeles Rams defense lines up pre-snap during an NFL football game against the Los Angeles Chargers. Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

"He's just quick," Dolac told the California Post. "He's got the reaction time. He's got everything. He's got the moves."

But the real victory isn't your name on the leaderboard, it's the unattended byproduct that comes from the competitions and excitement around the league itself.

Instead of retreating into their phones between meetings, players are challenging teammates, laughing, talking trash and building relationships that extend beyond their position groups.

"It's another way of competing other than on the field," Dolac said. "It's a way to kind of decompress as well."

"You're not just sitting around on your phone. You're still interacting with each other."

And for a Rams team that stumbled out to a 2-4 start before battling back to 4-4, those connections matter. Getty Images

The shadow boxing league joins playing cards, grounding walks, and date nights as ways to develop off the field chemistry and team bonding. There's also a precedent for this translating into something meaningful.

Last season, the Seattle Seahawks embraced their own shadow boxing craze, with players crediting the game for helping build relationships across position groups. That connectedness became part of the culture surrounding Seattle's eventual Super Bowl championship.

Throughout the season, easily over half the Rams locker room has participated. Getty Images

Nobody should confuse shadow boxing with blocking schemes or defensive adjustments. The Rams won't win a championship because Garrett can outmaneuver a teammate in a pointing contest.

But championships aren't built exclusively on Sundays, either. They're built through trust, relationships, and the willingness to fight and play for the guy standing next to you.

And for a Rams team that stumbled out to a 2-4 start before battling back to 4-4, those connections matter.

Stuard also revealed that the Rams shadow boxing league is about to get even more serious. The commissioner is planning a tournament next week, potentially featuring seeded matchups, wild cards, and first-round byes based on the rankings.What is this, the College Football Playoff?

If and when the tournament takes place, Garrett's championship crown could finally be on the line.

Good luck to whomever draws that matchup.

#2897. Layne Riggs Reels From NASCAR Truck Title Loss But Looks Forward

HOMESTEAD, Fla. -- Layne Riggs came up short in the 2026 NASCAR Craftsman Truck Series championship but kept things in perspective after a standout season.

Riggs earned seven wins, 13 top-five finishes and 15 top-10 finishes in 25 races. He led nearly 1,000 laps (988) and averaged around an 11th-place finish (10.96). Before this season, he had only won five times and led 663 laps in 54 Truck starts before 2026.

Riggs had the No. 1 seed for the Chase. Right out of the gate, Riggs looked strong and led 135 of 234 completed laps in the Chase opener at Bristol Motor Speedway before he and his pit crew had issues in the final stage. They finished 32nd.

Riggs improved the next week in the final stage, putting himself in contention to win at Kansas Speedway. However, over-aggression to go for the win cost Riggs as he hit the wall and finished 19th.

Down the stretch, Riggs cleaned up his act -- winning at Charlotte, finishing third at Phoenix, eighth at Talladega and was in a position at Martinsville to sweep the stages and win the race overall. However, he lost out on entering the season finale in a tie with Honeycutt after losing the lead on the final restart -- finishing third.

Riggs will be back for another season in Trucks in 2027 with the possibility of running some NASCAR Cup Series races. No plans are set yet, however.

While Riggs didn't bring home the hardware for the championship, another driver brought home the hardware for his start in the sport.

Brenden "Butterbean" Queen finished 20th after starting 21st in Friday night's race but clinched Rookie of the Year honors for the season.

Queen earned five top-10 finishes and an average finish of 16.6. While his average finish ranked middle of the pack, it was around seven positions better than his average start.

Queen also finished highest among the Kaulig Racing Ram trucks, ranking 13th in the final driver's standings. He earned 18 more points than his next-closest full-time teammate (Justin Haley: 496, Queen: 514). Corey LaJoie finished 21st (363 points in 23 starts) and Mini Tyrrell finished 22nd (359 points) in the final standings.

While Tyrrell isn't coming back, Queen is looking ahead to the 2026 season. Kaulig CEO Chris Rice said they were prepping at Homestead-Miami with five different approaches

LaJoie appeared to have the best approach, finishing 10th in the season finale.

#2895. Las Vegas Raiders-San Francisco 49ers FEED Topic: Who gets more touches between Mike Washington Jr., Ashton Jeanty?

Las Vegas Raiders head coach Klint Kubiak made headlines during last week's game against the New York Jets. Not for himself, but a decision he made with Las Vegas' backfield.

Kubiak didn't just switch the Raiders' lead running back between rookie Mike Washington Jr. and second-year pro Ashton Jeanty; he turned Washington into the feature back with 25 carries and made Jeanty the third-down option with just two carries and three catches.

Now, heading into the Week 9 matchup against the San Francisco 49ers, the question arises of whether the workload split between Washington and Jeanty will continue.

Some have speculated that Kubiak's decision was punitive-based. Ahead of last week's game, Jeanty gave the Jets 'bulletin board material' by saying that a few players on New York's defense "don't really want to tackle". While the first-time head coach is still establishing his identity, he's made it clear that he takes a 'no-nonsense' type of approach and doesn't divulge much to the media to avoid situations like this. So, there's reason to believe that the running back's comments irked Kubiak and played a role in the former's workload last Sunday.

However, this Sunday will give us a better answer to see if the 25-to-2 (or 12.5-to-1) ratio of carries, or a similar type of split, continues. On Monday, the head coach indicated that the workload between the two backs could vary game to game.

"I think it's always a moving target," he replied when asked if the Raiders will have a shared backfield moving forward. "See where your team's at, and who's playing the best ball at the time. And it's a 17-game regular season where things change every week. And they've been changing at guard…That's the NFL.

"We have a young roster, and we're going to give guys opportunities and see what fits us best. But your 11, whether you're playing really well or not playing well at all, it's always going to change a little bit."

Despite those comments, it's hard to deny that the Raiders' rushing attack did a better job of staying on schedule last week.

Washington had 83 yards on his 25 carries, which comes out to 3.3 ypc. That's pretty comparable to Jeanty's 3.1 ypc for the season. But the rookie's average was brought down by a handful of stuffed runs near the goal line. Also, Vegas' EPA per rush was 0.11 points better last week than the team's season average, and its success rate on the ground was 7.2% higher, per Sumer Sports.

That being said, Washington's lack of success in the red zone can't be ignored. He's averaging 1.2 ypc (17 attempts for 21 yards) inside the 20-yard line, per Sumer, and is viewed as more of a home-run hitter out of the backfield. Granted, Jeanty isn't much better in the scoring area — 1.6 ypc (22 attempts for 36 yards) — but it is an improvement on a slightly bigger sample size. Also, he's arguably the better option at getting the tough yards of the two backs.

So, maybe Klint Kubiak's formula moving forward is to have the rookie help get the offense into the red zone and then have the second-year pro take over with fresh legs. Of course, we won't know the answer until Sunday's game, which is why the topic is this week's game day FEED discussion.

Meanwhile, the 49ers have a very good defense that is great at stopping the run. San Francisco ranks fifth in the NFL with 89.0 rushing yards allowed per game and is second in EPA per rush allowed at negative 0.17. Especially since the Jets were missing their best run defender last week, defensive tackle T'Vondre Sweat, this weekend will be a true test to see if the Raiders' rushing attack improves with Washington as the lead back.

So, for this week's game day discussion on The FEED, we'll be keeping tabs on how the carries are divided up in Las Vegas' backfield. The game kicks off at 1:25 p.m. PST and will air on FOX (check your local listings). I look forward to talkin' some ball with y'all!

#2893. ‘Women Absolutely Love Him’ — Analyst Fires Back at Former ESPN Star’s Criticism of Pat McAfee Over Utah Controversy

Former ESPN host Katie Nolan recently took Pat McAfee to task over his reaction to criticism from fans, particularly how he handled the fallout from College GameDay's visit to Utah. She argued that despite all his success, McAfee often acts like he's being unfairly targeted and expects others to look after his emotional well-being.

Her comments didn't sit well with everyone, though. While some agreed with Nolan's assessment, others weren't so quick to side with her. Dan Dakich was one of them, and he had some strong words in defense of McAfee.

Speaking on the "Don't @ Me" podcast, Dakich pushed back against Nolan's portrayal of McAfee, arguing that the ESPN personality is widely liked, particularly by women. He also took several personal shots at Nolan, questioning her attitude toward McAfee and the way she talks about him.

"Women love Pat McAfee. I mean, they don't like him; they love him. Not little whiners like you, who think you're so cool. Little whiners like you detest… you go to toxic masculinity," Dakich said.

He then brought up his own experience with McAfee, explaining that the two live near each other and sometimes cross paths at the same country club. He used that story to push back on Nolan's suggestion that McAfee's public image and behavior have turned people against him.

"Pat McAfee lives about a mile away from me now. [He used to live] closer. His place is right over here. Pat comes over to the same country club I belong to, and women absolutely love him."

Dakich also brought up how well McAfee's shows have done on ESPN, arguing that the numbers speak for themselves.

"ESPN's ratings are through the roof compared to when he is there now, as opposed to when he wasn't there, and that includes women," he said.

All things said, "College GameDay" has been drawing a big audience. ESPN said its Oct. 17 broadcast from Provo averaged 3 million viewers, the third-most in the show's history at the time.

"College GameDay" visited Salt Lake City on Oct. 24 ahead of Utah's game against Houston. McAfee later said he felt uncomfortable and unsafe there, going on to share screenshots of threatening messages he said he had received.

Nolan wasn't impressed with how he handled the situation and addressed it on the "Casuals with Katie Nolan" show.

She argued that McAfee can't put himself in the public eye and then expect everyone to go easy on him when the reaction isn't so positive. Nolan acknowledged how successful he's been but questioned why he seems to take negative attention so personally.

"The guy is winning in every sense of the word, and yet is constantly acting like the world is attacking him baselessly… He acts irresponsibly but then wants everybody to be responsible for his emotional well-being and to take care of him,"Nolan said.

Nolan also said she used to think McAfee was comfortable playing the heel, which made his reaction to the Utah crowd surprising to her.

#2890. Klopp settles Germany's goalkeeping debate ahead of November fixtures

Germany head coach Jürgen Klopp named three goalkeepers in his squad for the upcoming UEFA Nations League fixtures against Serbia and the Netherlands, which was announced on Thursday: Marc-André ter Stegen (Ajax), Jonas Urbig (Bayern Munich) and Alexander Nübel (Besiktas).

After rotating between the three in his first four matches in charge last month, Klopp has now confirmed that ter Stegen remains Germany's clear first-choice goalkeeper.

“Marc is having a fantastic season in Amsterdam, and fortunately, the other two [Urbig and Nübel] are also having really good seasons. We're going into these matches with Marc as our number one,” Klopp said in an interview with DFB.tv (via iMiaSanMia).

"And this isn't about who happens to be making the better saves at any given moment, because goalkeepers need to provide a lot of stability and confidence. The number one requirement for a goalkeeper is to make sure the ball doesn't end up in the net. That's the most important thing. But beyond that, there are several other aspects that matter: How can he dictate our game, including when we're in possession? How does he handle these situations? And when we're defending with a high line, how early can he recognise those situations so that he can defend further up the pitch as a goalkeeper?

"It's a strange way of putting it, but essentially, it's about knowing when to come out and deal with the danger – not trying to dribble, but simply clearing the ball. All of these things are part of the job. Marc has the most experience in this area, and, yes, we're going into these matches with him as our number one."

Germany face Serbia away from home on November 13 before hosting the Netherlands three days later.

#2886. Why key stat provides optimism for Lakers’ defense

The Lakers aren't where they want to be defensively.

But they have reasons to be optimistic that they can be a lot better on that end of the floor.

The Lakers entered Friday's home game against the Trail Blazers ranked No. 16 in defensive rating according to Cleaning The Glass, which filters out garbage time.

Lakers guard Matisse Thybulle is one of the biggest reasons for the team forcing as many turnovers as it does. AP Photo/Scott Marshall

Not quite the "nasty" defensive team starting center Walker Kessler said the Lakers would be. Or even a "pretty good" defense, as coach JJ Redick put it, saying the team elevated its expectations for that end of the floor after preseason.

But the small sample of the Lakers' first nine games showed a defensive area that the team is prolific in that can help them maximize their defensive potential: forcing turnovers.

The Lakers entered their 10th game of the season ranked No. 2 in defensive turnover percentage at 18.8, just behind the Raptors (19.1%) at No. 1.

"It shows that we have a lot of potential," Kessler said. "A couple of things we gotta clean up, but it shows that we're willing to be aggressive and turn guys over, especially in the half court."

The Lakers entered Friday ranked No. 4 in steals per game. AP Photo/Scott Strazzante

The Lakers entered Friday having forced at least 14 turnovers in every game they played, including 16 or more in seven of those nine games.

It has provided a spark to their league-best offense, with the Lakers leading the league in points scored off turnovers (23.4).

"Got guys that fly around, play the defensive end the right way and are in the right spots," Austin Reaves said. "Matisse [Thybulle], [Ziaire Williams], Jake [LaRavia], [Quentin Grimes], you can go down the line. So good defensive players that are in the right spot at the right time that stick to our schemes."

Luka Doncic (77) defends Giannis Antetokounmpo. NBAE via Getty Images

The Lakers entered Friday ranked No. 8 in half-court defense, in part, because they don't have to compromise their defense to create turnovers.

"I've talked about this before as it relates to giving offensive players freedom and creativity as long as it's done within our system and our rules and our structure: It's no different defensively," Redick said. "[We] want to disrupt, but we want to disrupt within our schemes. So, you can be a defensive playmaker by doing our defense versus gambling — there's a big difference.

"You've got to give those guys freedom, too."

Forcing turnovers isn't the be-all, end-all of good defensive teams.

Six of the top-12 defensive teams from last season were also top 12 in defensive turnover percentage. And it was seven of 12 the previous year.

The Lakers have other areas they need to clean up to make their defense pop: defensive rebounding (especially when Kessler isn't on the floor) and their own turnovers (which leads to easier scoring opportunities for opponents) are among them.

But they've found an aspect of defense to be proud over during the first 2 ½ weeks of the season.

#2882. Nick Sirianni admits that he misled reporters about quarterback situation

How do you know when a football coach is lying? If he’s good at it, you don’t.

Because they all do. Particularly in so-called “must-lie” situations.

There’s an art to it. A way to conceal a lie with another lie. On Friday, Eagles coach Nick Sirianni opted not to lie about the fact that he lied on Monday when he told reporters that quarterback Tanner McKee would not be benched.

“Tanner’s our guy,” Sirianni said. “We’re not making any changes there.”

On Wednesday, the Eagles made a change there, elevating Joe Milton over McKee. The move was announced after Sirianni had his midweek media availability, which prevented him from being asked questions about the change in position from “Tanner’s our guy” to “Tanner’s not our guy.”

On Friday, Sirianni finally faced the music regarding his comments from Monday. In lieu of coming up with an explanation premised on the idea that what he said was true when he said it and circumstances changed, Sirianni surprisingly admitted that he wasn’t telling the truth, the whole truth, and nothing but the truth to reporters and, in turn, the fans.

“I was going to hold onto that as long as I possibly could, every second, every minute,” Sirianni said. “Again, I’m not real shy with you guys. I’m going to tell you what I want to tell you guys. I respect the job that y’all have to do, but I’m going to tell you what I want to tell you guys because I’m always going to protect the team first and foremost.

“Every second that goes by in a situation like that, that you can hold onto information as long as you possibly can, is a little less time for the opponent to get ready for the game. I know there’s a lot of interest in everything that you all want to hear, the fans want to hear. My job is to win football games, do everything we can do to help win football games, protect the guys here on this football team, and handle everything that I can inside.”

In an industry where the truth is only told when it’s coincidental to strategic interests, it’s jarring that Sirianni told the truth that he was lying. It would have been easy to say, “We took a more careful look at the situation after Monday.” Or, even more simply, “We changed our mind. Shit happens.”

Instead, Sirianni opted to say, essentially, “Bullshit happens.” Instead of bothering to concoct a cover story regarding his lie, he said, essentially, “Yeah. I lied. I’ll do it whenever I feel like I have to.”

That’s the most important thing to remember. Coaches lie whenever they feel like they have to. Despite that fact, reporters and fans routinely take their words at face value, swallowing the bullshit while saying, “Thank you, sir, may I have another?”

So thank you, Nick, for making my job a little easier. In my first week of law school, one of the professors said, “The most important thing I can do for you is to give you a good crap filter.”

Because crap is everywhere. Usually, the people spewing it don’t admit it. When they do, it provides a tangible reminder as to whatever they say in the future.

For Sirianni, that’s the most problematic aspect of his decision to tell the truth about lying. There’s now an “Exhibit A” to which anyone and everyone can point as to anything and everything he says when assessing whether he’s being straight with reporters and, in turn, with fans.

#2877. LeBron James gets warm welcome in Cleveland before Cavaliers rout 76ers

LeBron James might have chosen to join the Philadelphia 76ers instead of returning to the Cleveland Cavaliers, but it was all love in "The Land."

James and the 76ers took on the Cavaliers in Cleveland for an NBA Cup game on Friday, Nov. 6. and the atmosphere was warm and welcoming for the 24-year veteran and Ohio native.

In the tunnel to the locker room, there was a video board that said "Welcome Home LeBron!" with an image of him holding up Cleveland's 2016 NBA Finals trophy. During the Prime pregame show, a young fan held up James' St. Vincent–St. Mary High School jersey, and other fans were shown wearing Cavaliers jerseys with James' name on them.

Just before tipoff, James did his signature chalk toss and fans were crowding around with their phones to record the moment. A video from ESPN NBA reporter Dave McMenamin on X showed that as the 76ers were being announced, Jaylen Brown and Joel Embiid received boos but James was greeted with cheers.

All of that happened before the Cavaliers smacked Philadelphia 119-95. They held their hometown hero to eight points, and James committed six turnovers. The loss snapped the 76ers' five-game winning streak. James' eight points also snapped his own 396-game streak of scoring 10+ points in regular-season games in Cleveland, the "NBA on ESPN" social media account reported.

James has now gone three years without beating teh Cavaliers in Cleveland. According to the Prime pregame show, James hasn't beat the Cavaliers in their home arena since November 25, 2023 when the Lakers won 121-115, when he had 22 points, six assists and six rebounds.

This offseason, James informed the Los Angeles Lakers that he was going to leave the team. The Cavaliers were among the teams that the NBA's all-time leading scorer was considering. The 76ers won the battle with James crediting Tyrese Maxey for helping him make his decision.

James' 76ers teammate Dean Wade also joined Philadelphia this offseason after spending the first seven seasons of his career in Cleveland. He spoke to the media ahead of the game about his memories facing James in his hometown.

"In my seven years, anytime that LeBron came back it was just a madhouse. It was crazy," he said in a video posted by McMenamin on X. "The fans were awesome. So I expect that to be what tonight's going to be like. Super loud, a lot of energy. I think it's gonna be a fun night."

James grew up in Akron, Ohio and joined the Cavaliers out of high school as the No.1 overall pick in the 2003 draft. He spent the first seven seasons of his career in Cleveland before leaving to join the Miami Heat in "The Decision" in 2010. After winning two championships in South Beach, he returned for the 2014-15 season. He guided the Cavaliers to their first NBA title in 2016 when he was named Finals MVP. James left again in 2018 to join the Lakers and won his fourth championship in 2020.

#2876. Brandon Aiyuk plans to meet with 49ers at team's business offices, not team facility

49ers tight end George Kittle was right, sort of. Receiver Brandon Aiyuk won’t “get in” the team facility on Friday, because he’s not trying to.

Per agent Drew Rosenhaus, Friday’s meeting will happen at the team’s business offices and not at the team’s practice facility.

That makes the session not a formal reporting-for-duty by Aiyuk, in the hopes of forcing the team’s hand. As explained earlier today, the NFL still hasn’t reinstated Aiyuk from the reserve/left squad list — even though Rosenhaus filed the request on October 27.

Instead, it’s a meeting presumably aimed at negotiating a resolution to the situation.

The 49ers pushed to reporters on Thursday night the idea that Aiyuk’s return to the NFL is tied to the $13 million the team believes he owes in previously paid signing bonus. That shouldn’t be part of the analysis as to whether he’s reinstated by the league, whether the 49ers put him on the roster again, and/or whether the 49ers cut him.

But they’re making it an issue, because they can tie his ability to play to his willingness to wire the disputed funds to the team.

So that’s what’s happening. The brakes already have been applied to what should have been an administrative and automatic process. The league and the 49ers are working together to help the team get money back from Aiyuk before Aiyuk is permitted to get back into the league.

Then there’s the fact that an active arrest warrant is pending for Aiyuk, arising from a speeding incident. The business meeting could potentially be interrupted by an attempt to enforce a specific item of police business.

#2875. Fantasy Bundesliga tips: 10 players to consider for Matchday 9

In this Fantasy Bundesliga article, we’ll highlight 10 players to consider adding to your team ahead of Matchday 9.

As a rule of thumb, we'll try to steer clear of the most obvious picks, instead focusing mainly on players who could offer great value as differentials.

Koki Machida, Hoffenheim (Defender, 4.1M, 5%)

Coming up against a Schalke side who have managed just four Bundesliga goals this season, Machida offers solid clean-sheet potential at a budget-friendly price.

Miro Muheim, Hamburger SV (Defender, 9.95M, 8%)

Operating out of position in central midfield, Muheim has already shown his explosive potential this season, racking up 315 points against Hoffenheim and 289 against VfB Stuttgart. With HSV hosting bottom-placed Gladbach, the 28-year-old looks like an intriguing differential for Friday night's fixture.

Nathaniel Brown, FC Bayern (Defender, 14.85M, 9%)

After coming off the bench against both Borussia Dortmund and Atletico Madrid, Brown looks set to return to the starting XI against Bayern this weekend, with the German champions expected to rotate their squad amid a packed fixture schedule. The German international could even feature in a more advanced role on the left wing.

Albert Grønbæk, Hamburger SV (Midfielder, 6.99M, 1%)

Although Hamburger SV lost 3-1 to Eintracht Frankfurt last week, Grønbæk still managed to pick up a respectable 122 points. The Danish international operates in a very advanced role – almost as a striker – and with HSV now facing a much more favourable home fixture against a Gladbach side yet to win away from home this season, he's an interesting midfield option at a relatively low price.

Can Uzun, Eintracht Frankfurt (Midfielder, 15.94M, 2%)

Following criticism from head coach Adi Hütter over his mentality and two consecutive starts on the bench, Uzun has worked his way back into the Eintracht line-up and really delivered. He was arguably their standout performer against HSV last weekend and could be influential once more against newly-promoted Paderborn.

Lennart Karl, FC Bayern (Midfielder, 16.34M, 2%)

After coming under criticism for his early-season performances, Karl responded in style with a crucial goal in Bayern's 2-0 win over Atletico Madrid on Tuesday. With Bayern dealing with a lengthy injury list in attack, the 18-year-old looks set to retain his place in the No. 10 role against Mainz, offering an opportunity to build on his impressive mid-week performance.

Konstantinos Karetsas, Borussia Dortmund (Midfielder, 15.18M, 4%)

The Greek youngster gave Dortmund a real boost off the bench in their 2-1 win over Real Betis on Wednesday, scoring the winner. After that performance, you'd expect Karetsas to start this weekend's favourable fixture against Elversberg, but be aware that his minutes could still be managed following his recent injury spell.

Fabio Silva, Borussia Dortmund (Striker, 9.52M, 1%)

Silva built on his goal against Bayern Munich by scoring and providing an assist against Real Betis in the Champions League on Wednesday. Since Serhou Guirassy picked up a knock in that game, the striker even looks likely to play as the front man at home against newly-promoted Elversberg.

Fisnik Asllani, Hoffenheim (Striker, 12.63M, 1%)

Asllani appears to be the most likely starter in Hoffenheim's attack as Christian Ilzer's side look to pick up three much-needed points against struggling Schalke 04 this weekend. The 24-year-old is still working his way back to his best after a spell on the sidelines, but is certainly capable of making a difference, as he showed last season with 11 goals and 10 assists in all competitions.

Igor Matanovic, Freiburg (Striker, 16.59M, 14%)

Union Berlin have conceded heavily in several away fixtures this season, suffering defeats to Leverkusen (4-0), Bayern (7-0) and Köln (5-0). Matanovic will be hoping to exploit the defensive frailties the capital club have shown this season and add to his tally of four Bundesliga goals at the weekend.

Garrett Wilson of the New York Jets looks on during a game against the Miami Dolphins in East Rutherford, N.J., Sunday, Oct. 25, 2026. Getty Images

New York Jets wide receiver Adonai Mitchell (15) stretches during practice in Florham Park, N.J., Friday, Oct. 23, 2026. Bill Kostroun for NY Post

#2868. Brandon Ingram is 'pretty close' to making debut with L.A. Clippers: Latest injury update

Brandon Ingram is working his way back and is close to his debut with the Los Angeles Clippers.

The two-time All-Star has been the biggest absence for the team this season, considering he will be an immediate starter upon his return.

He is recovering from right heel surgery he had in May. He is also dealing with a partially torn Achilles tendon.

"I'm pretty close," Ingram told reporters during media availability on Friday. "I've been on the floor a little bit more than I've been in the past. I didn't have a summer to have 5-on-5, move at all, but I'm gaining that confidence right now."

The Clippers acquired Ingram as one of the primary trade assets in the package sent over by the Toronto Raptors for Kawhi Leonard during the offseason.

Ingram told reporters he was initially supposed to have a bone removed from his heel, but the doctors found the partially torn Achilles during that procedure. While sidelined, he's remained close to the team and appreciates the players' efforts.

"I actually want to commend those guys because everybody is in new positions and asked to do more," Ingram said. "So the guys are trying. They're playing really, really good defense."

Ingram's return would be welcomed, especially on the offensive end of the floor. The All-Star led Toronto with 21.5 points and was third in rebounds (5.6) last season. He started 77 games for the Raptors.

"They're trying to figure it out on the offensive end," Ingram said. "When I come in, I want to play make, make it a little bit easier for guys to catch and shoot, and not have to make so many decisions. And be able to, when it gets down to it, be able to score."

Ingram already had a level of familiarity with head coach Tyronn Lue and assistant coach Jeff Van Gundy from playing for Team USA during the 2023 FIBA World Cup.

Ingram, center Yanic Konan Niederhäuser and Max Strus, who has the ability to play as both a guard and forward, were recalled on Nov. 2. The trio participated in a weekend assignment with the team's G-League affiliate.

Konan Niederhäuser is working his way back from right Lisfranc surgery. Strus was recovering from a partial tear to his right plantar fascia.

"Max came back and had a big impact on the team," Ingram said. "He's made the team better. Yanic is coming back soon.

"... As a full team, I think we're gonna mesh well. And we'll be a little better than we have been. But in these games that we lost, we've been right in the game. Just fix a few mistakes and we can win those games."

The Clippers won the season opener against the Sacramento Kings before losing seven straight. Los Angeles will host the New Orleans Pelicans on Nov. 8.

#2867. Rutgers overcomes early air balls to blow out New Haven, secure first win for acting head coach Brandin Knight

After watching his Scarlet Knights launch multiple air balls in the opening minutes of his first regular season game as acting head coach, Brandin Knight winced.

Rutgers was also allowing New Haven to knock down 3-pointers and secure offensive rebounds that led to second-chance points.

But with just over a minute remaining in the first half, Rutgers sprung to life when point guard Tariq Francis threw an alley-oop pass to backcourt mate Jamichael Davis, who finished it with flair. Next time down, Davis, a 6-foot-2 senior, hit a 3-pointer; the home crowd erupted.

"Unbelievable," Knight said. "Unbelievable. I don't know what ladder he stepped on."

That flourish put Rutgers up 15 points, and the lead ballooned to 30 points midway through the second half. The sequence proved to be the most important in Rutgers' 81-54 victory.

With head coach Steve Pikiell absent due to his ongoing battle with cancer, Knight secured his first win with a roster that remains in transition and in need for repetitions together.

"Just thankful and grateful that everyone showed up," Knight said. "It felt electric for the first night. My happiness comes in how we do as a team."

Rutgers, which finished last season 14-20, will play NJIT at home on Tuesday.

Francis and Davis spurred the win as a frentic force in the backcourt: Francis, a volume shooter, finished with 14 points on 5 of 14 shooting, including 1 of 6 from beyond the arc. Davis (13 points) was a perfect 5 of 5 from the field after coming off the bench.

"I saw him open coming out of the corner of my eye," Francis said of how the alley oop developed. "I knew if I threw, he could get it and he did."

Their chemistry is evident when they bring a kinetic pace to the fastbreak.

On defense, the duo complements each other well, too. Both were active in the passing lanes, with Davis forcing a turnover immediately upon entering the game. He also had 3 of the team's 10 steals as the unit will lean on him for veteran leadership and ballhandling savvy throughout the season.

Greetings, Gurdak: In the paint, center Christian Gurdak, who transferred to Piscataway from Virginia Tech, picked up early fouls, but he still managed to contribute 12 points on 4 of 6 shooting in 17 minutes. He hit all four of his free throws, grabbed three offensive boards and stole the ball twice. He settled in defensively to finish the game with three fouls.

Each member of the Rutgers interior rotation had a moment. First it was Gurdak with a banker; then it was Mervedi Miteo, a 6-foot-10 freshman from the Congo, powering his way to the basket. All their muscle and more will be needed in Big 10 play if the Scarlet Knights are to be competitive in conference.

Darin Smith Jr., a 6-foot-7 guard, showed he can be a source of rebounds when his offense isn't there. Though he struggled with two air balls early on, he posted 6 boards in his stat line by game's end.

Good energy from Rasheed Jones: The fifth year senior joined Rutgers as a graduate transfer after spending the last two seasons at Coastal Carolina, and he gave his team, as well as the fans, a needed jolt during lulls in the game. First he hit a three; then, when Rutgers missing shots, he broke out for an easy dunk. He added a corner three in the second half and added 14 points while also goading the crowd to get more involved.

Defense, defense, defense: It is the side of the ball that Knight has long been known for dating back to his playing days at Pitt, and Rutgers will need to excel on that end to pick up victories as the offense gets figured out.

On the first possession, the Scarlet Knights set the tone by forcing a shot clock violation. Knight also mixed up the looks and deployed full-court pressure to speed up New Haven's decision making. Rutgers created 17 turnovers and capitalized by converting those takeaways into 24 points.

But lapses around the arc allowed New Haven to hit five 3-pointers. And rebounding remains a work in progress.

Both Teshaun Steele and Zoumana Traore had 15 points for New Haven.

#2864. Rams Reacts: Do you think Sean McVay is part of the problem?

As the once perceived 17-0 Los Angeles Rams sit at 4-4, someone needs to be blamed, and according to fans surveyed, Sean McVay deserves more than his fair share. Over 67% the fans polled blame McVay as being a "part of the problem" for the team's struggles, as opposed to around 32% expressing that McVay is "not a problem".

To go so far as to say the Rams could do better at head coach than McVay may be foolhardy, but to say that McVay could be doing a better job as his team sits 4-4? That seems fair. McVay would likely agree, he could be doing a better job.

Of course, coaching only gets you so far, yet, in the NFL coaching may be more important than any other sport. McVay is one of the best NFL coaches we've seen. Even if he never wins another game, what he's done since entering the league in 2017 has changed the NFL.

Despite the historical relevance McVay has at the NFL level, football is a "What have you done for me lately business?" Lately, McVay has helped guide his team from 2-4 to 4-4.

If he keeps winning, if the Rams keeps winning, the fans will believe, and McVay will be seen as a solution, not a problem.

Speaking of belief in the Rams, how confident are you that this team is headed in the right direction? According to fans surveyed, their confidence in this team is a coin flip with 51% of fans saying this team is headed the right way, while 49% are not so positive.

Coaching aside, the Rams on paper, despite with the loss of Jaylen Watson, have a stronger roster than the Commanders. The Rams should win even if McVay doesn't bring his A-game on Sunday. Though, games aren't played on paper, otherwise the Rams would be 17-0 instead of 4-4.

Jayden Daniels in terms of talent, has it. He can give the Rams fits if they don't contain him. But for the second time this year, Myles Garrett should be suiting up for the Rams, and perhaps he'll have a better showing than Week 1 and actually earn a sack, or a tackle, or anything. Garrett doesn't have any real stats for L.A. after his lone game against the San Francisco 49ers, which didn't go well for the entire team.

Maybe Week 9 is L.A. rookie CJ Daniels' chance to breakout, maybe Kyren Williams and Blake Corum each score, or maybe Washington's own rookie Sonny Styles puts down a sack or two. If you want to bet on it, FanDuel should have you covered.

McVay and the Rams can change the tone of their season with a win on Sunday to get to 5-4. A loss, and L.A.'s season may officially be spiraling. If you feel any type of way about the Rams or another team, check out FanDuel.

Filipe, who rushed for more than 1,500 yards this season, added a 1-yard score with 5:24 left to seal the win. Despite a promising start by Davis, including Jaxton Itaaheau's two touchdown receptions, the Darts couldn't keep pace. Herriman advances, aiming to surpass last year's quarterfinal finish.

No. 3 Corner Canyon (10-2) continued its dominance over No. 11 Syracuse (6-5) with a commanding 42-6 win in the 6A quarterfinals. The Chargers, seeking their fourth consecutive state title under first-year coach Casey Sutera, established control early with Carter Kjar's 43-yard touchdown reception from Jaxon Pehrson just over nine minutes into the game. Pehrson, who totaled 32 touchdowns this season, added a 4-yard rushing score in the third quarter, contributing to Corner Canyon's 28-0 halftime lead. Jackson Carruth, the team's leading rusher, scored twice on the ground. Syracuse's lone score came late in the third quarter, a 45-yard pass from Ledger Wight to Bo Metcalfe.

In a commanding 42-3 victory, No. 2 Mountain Ridge (12-0) showcased their potent offense and stifling defense against No. 7 Lone Peak (6-6) in the 6A quarterfinals. Mountain Ridge's Jaxon Hunt threw for three touchdowns, notably connecting with Tytan DeJong twice, including an 8-yard score with just 19 seconds left in the first half. The Sentinels' defense held Lone Peak scoreless until the fourth quarter, extending their dominance this season with a 45.9 points-per-game average, the best in 6A. Mountain Ridge, undefeated and seeking its first state title, advances to face the Corner Canyon in the semifinals.

Skyridge (11-1) held off No. 8 seed American Fork (7-5) in a closely contested 6A quarterfinal game, winning 28-21. The top-seeded Falcons solidified the victory in the fourth quarter with a pair of touchdowns, including a pivotal 31-yard pass from quarterback Jagger Fountaine to Dax Rehrer with 3:31 remaining. Fountaine was integral to the Falcons' offense, contributing three total touchdowns — two passing and one rushing. American Fork, led by Nate Childs, attempted a late rally with a 21-yard touchdown connection to Cash Taiese with 1:55 left in the game, but Skyridge's defense held firm. Eli Mortensen was a standout for Skyridge, scoring on an 18-yard run and a long 74-yard reception.

Top-seeded West (12-0) dominated No. 8 Fremont (7-5) with a 49-0 victory in the 5A quarterfinals, maintaining their perfect record. West's stifling defense extended its shutout streak to five games, as Fremont struggled to break through. Naeata Hamilton opened the scoring with a 4-yard touchdown run at 10:34 in the first quarter. Star quarterback Kamden Lopati added a 20-yard rushing touchdown and later connected with Monson Vaenuku and Dovani Dixon for touchdown passes. Fremont's offense, led by Calvin Cooper, was unable to penetrate West's top-ranked defense, which also scored through Dieter Lotulelei's 15-yard fumble recovery.

No. 4 Bountiful (9-2) edged out No. 5 Springville (8-3) with a 23-17 victory in the 5A quarterfinals. Bountiful secured the win with Manu Tia's 2-yard touchdown run with 10:02 remaining in the fourth quarter, giving the Redhawks a late 23-14 lead. The Redhawks' defense, which hadn't allowed a point over the past three games, clinched the win by holding Springville to a late field goal with 3:31 left. Hudson Malmrose contributed with two touchdown passes, including one to Easton Lowe in the third quarter. Springville's Siaosi "Leka" Toki caught a 32-yard touchdown from Beckett Burton, but the Red Devils couldn't overcome Bountiful's strong defensive effort, marking Bountiful's ninth consecutive win and a spot in the 5A semifinals.

No. 14 Woods Cross (6-6) stunned No. 6 Alta (8-4) in the 5A quarterfinals with a thrilling 31-24 victory despite a late rally by the Hawks in the fourth quarter. Woods Cross established a strong lead early, with Tage Ita contributing two rushing touchdowns and a passing touchdown, pushing the Wildcats to a 24-7 advantage by halftime. Bronson Martin's 14-yard touchdown run in the third quarter extended their lead to 31-7. Alta then mounted a comeback, scoring 17 unanswered points in the fourth quarter. Xavier Cocci's 4-yard touchdown pass to Lucas Pagel and his own 1-yard run helped narrow the gap, and Madden McCallister's 25-yard field goal with 3:12 remaining tightened the score to 31-24. However, Woods Cross held on for the win, securing their first state semifinal appearance since Brody Benson became head coach.

In the 5A quarterfinals, No. 2 seed Orem (9-2) overcame a sluggish start to defeat No. 10 Pleasant Grove (7-5) by a score of 35-16, advancing to the semifinals. Despite trailing 13-0 after two Ben Lyman field goals and a Corbin Robbins touchdown run, Orem responded with a 40-yard touchdown pass from Tanner Lewis to Brooks Ross with just 54 seconds left in the first half. Lewis, who has thrown 20 touchdowns this season with just one interception, continued his impressive play, throwing two more touchdown passes in the second half to Major Stokes and Po Faumui. Orem sealed the victory with a 14-yard interception return for a touchdown by Aoi Faumui in the final minutes. The Tigers, who've been averaging over 35 points per game, relied on their potent offense and opportunistic defense to secure their eighth consecutive win.

Josh Pinnock's 40-yard field goal with two seconds left in the fourth quarter propelled No. 6 Skyline (9-3) past No. 14 Provo (7-5), 23-20, in the 4A quarterfinals. The Eagles, seeking their first state title since 2005, advance to the semifinals thanks to a balanced offensive attack led by Tate Johnson's 20-yard touchdown pass to Will Black earlier in the fourth quarter. Johnson finished with two touchdown passes. Provo, who started the season 0-3, rallied to take the lead with Teave Brown's 3-yard scoring run early in the final quarter but ultimately couldn't hold on.

In the 4A quarterfinals, No. 1 Ridgeline (12-0) dominated No. 8 Highland (8-3) with a 50-24 victory, extending their unbeaten streak to 25 games. The RiverHawks quickly took control, scoring 22 points in the first quarter, highlighted by Noah Fiefia's two rushing touchdowns and Crew Cox's connection with Owen Leishman for two scores. By halftime, Ridgeline built a commanding 50-7 lead, with Fiefia adding another touchdown to his school-record, 41-touchdown season. Highland's Viliamu Ripley managed two touchdowns, but the gap was too large to overcome. Ridgeline's victory sets them up for a semifinal matchup as it aims for a second consecutive state championship.

No. 2 Hurricane (11-1) edged out a dramatic 29-28 overtime victory against No. 7 Salem Hills (9-3) in the 4A quarterfinals, thanks to Titan Borchardt's decisive 11-yard touchdown run coupled with Drake Gines' successful two-point conversion. Borchardt, who rushed for 1,231 yards this season, added a crucial receiving touchdown late in the second quarter, while Kannon Cowan threw for two touchdowns. Salem Hills mounted an impressive rally, scoring 21 unanswered points in the fourth quarter, led by Cash Hollingshead's aerial attack. Joe Hughes caught three touchdown passes, including a 41-yard strike with 4:49 left, sending the game into overtime where Salem Hills' efforts fell just short despite Hughes opening the scoring.

No. 5 Crimson Cliffs (9-2) advanced to the 4A semifinals with a 28-14 victory over No. 4 Snow Canyon (7-4), securing the win with a late score. Tanoai Andresen connected with Noah Whitmore for an 89-yard touchdown early in the first quarter, establishing a lead the Mustangs would not relinquish. Andresen threw four touchdown passes, including strikes to Colt Bastian and James Wintch, with the team maintaining pressure throughout. Parker Olson's two touchdown passes for Snow Canyon tied the game early but couldn't spark a comeback. Crimson Cliffs' defense, allowing just 13.9 points per game, held firm, continuing their dominance in the all-time series against Snow Canyon.

#2862. Draymond Green says Warriors have 'moved on' after Kristaps Porziņģis steps back from playing: 'You can't sit and worry about spoiled milk'

With Golden State center Kristaps Porziņģis unexpectedly taking a step away from basketball due to health issues, Warriors veteran Draymond Green says the team is not dwelling on his exit.

When speaking to The Athletic's Sam Amick on Thursday, Green said the Warriors have never had a "culture" of blame and bickering, and spoke about moving forward from Porziņģis regardless of how helpful he would've been on the court.

"No one's sitting around bashing this guy or even worried about him, for that matter. In our minds, we've moved forward with the players that we have here," Green told The Athletic. "We know what type of player he is. But I don't think anyone in here is sitting around waiting on him to walk through that door. Everybody's moved on in their mindset."

Green told The Athletic that Warriors general manager Mike Dunleavy told the team about the ongoing Porzingis situation ahead of the team's season opener against the Los Angeles Lakers. The situation was re-addressed last week, with Green saying the team told players that it was "moving forward."

Green repeated that the Warriors are focusing on the chances for others to step up, with the veteran mentioning journeyman center Charles Bassey and undrafted rookie Graham Ike as players with something to prove.

"Like I said, everybody's moved on. It's a next man up mentality, right?" Green told The Athletic. "You embrace those guys, and you pour into those guys, to try to help them with all that they can be helped with so that they could ultimately help this team. But you can't sit and worry about spoiled milk."

Porziņģis, who joined the Warriors after being traded in February, has yet to play for Golden State this season while dealing with a health issue. The 31-year-old Latvian center signed a two-year, $40 million contract this summer to return to the Warriors, but has reportedly not been paid that salary while he is away from the team.

Though neither Porziņģis or the Warriors have clarified what health problems the center has been dealing with, Porziņģis has a history with postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome (POTS), a chronic condition that can affect the nervous system.

Without Porziņģis, Golden State is relying on Al Horford as a backup center behind Green, along with Bassey and Ike to fill out the depth.

Next week, the Chicago Bears, Denver Broncos, Philadelphia Eagles, and Tampa Bay Buccaneers have a bye. Combine that with this season's injuries, and the roster crunch is here. Who should you look to pick up now, before Sunday's games start?

Rodgers is on bye this week, and generally, fantasy players don't love the idea of picking up a "non-usable" player. However, this hasn't been a normal fantasy season. We have two top-flight quarterbacks, Jalen Hurts and Lamar Jackson, on injured reserve. We have four teams on bye next week, so we have to operate a bit differently this year. Rodgers is up against the Bengals after the bye, which naturally sets up for a higher-scoring game. He posted 17 fantasy points or more in four of his last six games, which is pretty darn solid.

Given the fact that the Rams are still without Davante Adams, there's a bit of an opening in the passing game.
