#2854. Celtics brutalize injury-riddled Hornets 119-80 for 2nd NBA Cup win

Jayson Tatum came back from a rest game with a head of steam, and the Charlotte Hornets stood no chance with five notable injuries.

Tatum's 34 points (24 of which he scored in the first half) led the Boston Celtics to a 119-80 win over the Hornets in the teams' first matchup of the season and Boston's first stop in a 4-game road trip. The win gives the C's a 7-2 record on the season and an NBA Cup group play record of 2-0 in East Group C.

Boston now leads the group by record, and sports a +53 scoring differential, which leads the group by 44 points. The winner of each group, plus two wild cards from each conference, will make it to the NBA Cup's knockout rounds.

The Celtics starting squad tonight included Derrick White, Jordan Walsh, Paul George, Jayson Tatum, and Neemias Queta. Boston's injury report was clear.

The Hornets started Coby White, Brandon Miller, Grayson Allen, Naz Reid, and Ryan Kalkbrenner. Kon Knueppel, Moussa Diabate, and former Celtics Grant Williams and Dennis Schroder, sat out with injuries. Miller also left the game at the half with right groin soreness.

Tatum started hot with an easy three-pointer off a screen, as the Hornets opened up the game with Reid guarding him. But he did not spam that matchup off the bat. Instead, the opening minutes were defined by energy and activity. George hit a three-pointer after Queta rebounded a midrange clunker. George and Queta both stuffed a couple Hornets shot attempts. White got a defensive rebound, ran the length of the floor and hit a transition 3 to give the C's a 9-2 lead three minutes into the game.

White and JT kept the momentum going for a few more minutes. White scored on back-to-back and-1s, including a 4-point play, to get 7 points in 15 seconds of game time. Tatum scored the next two buckets and put Boston up 21-7 with less than 7 minutes to go. At that point, the C's had connected on 5-7 three-point attempts.

However, the offense started dragging after that as the Hornets seemed to settle in. Payton Pritchard hit a sweet step-back three-pointer, and the Celtics' activity didn't slow, but shots stopped falling so effortlessly. In response, Coach Joe Mazzulla called a timeout and subbed in Baylor Scheierman and Sam Hauser to inject some offense. Scheierman immediately made an impact by splashing a three-pointer, then drew a foul on the Hornets to get the ball back for the C's soon after.

Luckily for the Celtics, Tatum stayed hot. He drove to the basket for a couple nice layups, got to the line and hit a pair of free throws, got an and-1 with another layup, then nailed a three-pointer. That wasn't the end of it, though. Before the quarter ended, he assisted on a deep (and I mean DEEP) Hauser three-pointer, and got the ball to Scheierman for an attempt from behind the arc at the buzzer. Scheierman missed the shot, but it was a quality look.

By the end of the opening frame, Tatum already had 18 points and Boston led Charlotte 40-20.

The C's stayed active in the second quarter despite the big lead. Mitchell Robinson opened the frame with a tough finish through contact inside (of course, he missed his free throw) and was all over the boards. Without Tatum, who regularly takes a rest at the start of second quarters, the team struggled to make its shots — but so did the Hornets. Both teams scored a combined 3 points over the first 3 minutes of the second quarter.

Boston began to break out of that lull around the 8-minute mark of the second frame, when White hit a three-pointer. That shot seemed to take the lid off the basket for the C's, as George cashed in on a floater soon after. George then hit a three-pointer, which Luka Garza followed up on the next possession with another 3. Almost halfway through the quarter, the Celtics led 53-25.

JT came back into the game with 5:34 left to go in the second frame, and after about a minute, hit a beautiful step-back 3 to keep the offense rolling. It was the first of three straight offensive possessions with made 3s for the Celtics, as Pritchard and Tatum hit on the team's next 2 three-point attempts. Those buckets helped secure Boston's first 30-point lead of the game, as the C's led 62-31 with 3 minutes left to go in the half.

Naz Reid hit back-to-back shots to cut into the lead, but White hit a couple of his own, and Robinson got a block that led to an open Hauser 3, leaving the Celtics with an imposing 70-39 lead at the half. Boston won the quarter 30-19.

Jayson Tatum, who was red-hot in the first half, racked up 24 points on 57.1% shooting from the field and 5-8 shooting from three-point range. He also had 4 rebounds and 6 assists before the break.

Boston's second-leading scorer was Derrick White, who had 18 points, 4 rebounds, 5 assists, and a steal at the half. No other Celtic had more than 8 points, but the team as a whole shot 54.8% from three-point range.

On the other end of the court, Naz Reid led Charlotte in scoring at the half with 10 points. The Hornets shot only 28.3% from the field and 21.7% from three-point range in the first half.

The Celtics maintained their energetic defense from the first half, but the offense briefly stuttered to open the second. Nevertheless, Queta got the scoring going with a couple nice buckets, including a hook shot and an impressive one-handed alley-oop on a pass from JT. Tatum continued to make his mark on the game by hitting a couple free throws, then assisting on a tough Walsh dunk and-1. Four minutes into the third quarter, the C's had a 79-45 lead.

The Celtics didn't let themselves become complacent with such a lead. Tatum, Pritchard, and Scheierman kept the offense churning, while the defense stayed active and refused to allow Charlotte's offense to get in a flow. JT was unstoppable driving to the rim and reached 30 points on a layup with 4:26 remaining in the third. Robinson threw down a couple hefty alley-oops, the second of which put the game out of reach entirely, as the C's had a 94-53 lead with less than 2 minutes to go in the frame.

By the end of the third quarter, which the Celtics won 26-18, Boston led by 39 points.

The C's opened the fourth with Pritchard, Hauser, Hugo Gonzalez, Mike Conley, and Luka Garza on the floor. While the Hornets scored the first 4 points of the quarter, Hauser and Pritchard splashed a couple three-pointers that inspired Charlotte to call a timeout, raise the white flag, and send in its reserves. Boston subbed in Ron Harper Jr. for Pritchard, but otherwise kept the same squad on the court to run up the lead.

Without Pritchard, Gonzalez and RHJ led the Celtics offense. After Gonzalez hit a 3 with 5:35 to go in the final frame, the Celtics had a 112-67 lead. It was such an insurmountable lead that NBA.com's NBA Cup standings page showed Boston's group play record as 2-0 about halfway through the fourth quarter.

The C's offense was a little clunky the rest of the way, but a couple three-pointers from Walsh and Garza inflated the lead to 118-74 with about 2 minutes left in the game.

Tatum finished the game with 34 points on 56.3% shooting from the field and 11-11 free throws, 7 rebounds, 9 assists, and a steal.

White failed to score a single point in the second half, but still finished as Boston's second-leading scorer for the night, with 18 points. The only other Celtic to finish in double digits was Pritchard, who had 12 points.

The team as a whole shot 45.8% from the field and 54.5% from three-point range. Boston's 24 made three-pointers is a new NBA Cup record.

Reid led the Hornets with 14 points, while Sion James had 11 points off the bench. Overall, Charlotte shot 29.3% from the floor and 22% from behind the arc.

Boston's next game will be against the Milwaukee Bucks at 8 p.m. Sunday, Nov. 8, at the Fiserv Forum in Milwaukee. The road trip will then take the C's to Philadelphia and Toronto.

#2851. Argyle Liberty Christian sues to pause TAPPS ruling removing football team from playoffs

Argyle Liberty Christian filed a lawsuit Friday in an attempt to pause the TAPPS ruling this week that removed the school's football team from the playoffs.

"Earlier this week we moved quickly to retain world-class outside legal counsel to help us consider every possible legal option at our disposal to have the TAPPS ruling be paused or reversed," school president Blair McCullough wrote in a letter to parents. "In the name of basic fairness and decency, TAPPS has a duty to reconsider the clear and unambiguous evidence we provided them."

The letter, which Bill Jones of KTVT-TV (Channel 11) posted on social media, McCullough also wrote that one of Liberty Christian's legal options is to file a temporary restraining order to have a judge issue an immediate stay against the TAPPS ruling "until they can carefully consider the clear evidence we have provided them."

TAPPS has said little about the ruling, other than executive director Bryan Bunselmeyer providing a brief statement to The Dallas Morning News: "The way TAPPS works, we followed our policies, we followed our procedures and we had our best people make the decision."

At issue is whether Liberty Christian assistant Antonio Griffin coached wide receiver Jace Johnson on a 7-on-7 team. Johnson, who transferred from Abilene Cooper to Liberty Christian for the 2026 football season, was ruled ineligible Tuesday when the TAPPS executive board determined Liberty Christian violated the "follow the coach" rule and must forfeit the games Johnson played. Liberty Christian was the favorite to win the state championship before its removal.

In a news release, TAPPS said Liberty Christian athletic department leadership and football coaching staff "knew or should have known" about Johnson's connection to Griffin's Lionhearted 7-on-7 program before the season started.

Undra Hendrix II, a coach who works with Lionhearted, told The News he coaches Johnson's 18U 7-on-7 team and said Griffin is the owner but never coached. Hendrix said Johnson has played for him the last three years.

Liberty Christian parents say an email sent to TAPPS was tampered with and made to say the LionHearted Sports Collective is "Owned and Coached by Coach Griff." The parents claim they have the original email that was sent out and that it only says, "Owned by Coach Griff."

TAPPS has redrawn the eight-team TAPPS Division I playoff bracket and moved the start of that division's games back a week, so first-round games aren't scheduled to start until next week. TCA-Addison was added to the playoffs after Liberty Christian's removal.

#2845. NFC North has 17-3 record against non-NFC North teams

The NFC North is the best division in the NFL. As evidenced by how the teams of the NFC North have performed against the rest of the league.

The Vikings, Bears, Lions, and Packers have a combined record of 17-3 against non-NFC North teams. Via NBC Sports research, the ensuing 85 percent success rate is the highest by any division in the NFL since the 1970 merger.

In theory, all four teams of the NFC North could make the playoffs. To do that, however, each would have to finish higher in the wild-card pecking order than the Rams and the Seahawks (assuming the 49ers win the NFC West).

This week, two teams of the NFC North have an easier path to victory than the others; the Bears host the Buccaneers and the Lions visit the Dolphins.

For the Packers (who play the Patriots in New England) and the Vikings (who play the Bills on Monday night), it will be harder to keep pushing 17-3 to 21-3.

#2843. Drew Rosenhaus after Brandon Aiyuk's meeting with 49ers: "To be continued"

It has become abundantly clear that the NFL is delaying the reinstatement of 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk from the reserve/left squad list. It has also become abundantly clear that the 49ers hope to squeeze some cash out of Aiyuk before the league lets him back in.

It’s no surprise, then, that Friday’s sit-down between Aiyuk, agent Drew Rosenhaus, and the 49ers did not resolve the situation.

“Hey, guys,” Rosenhaus told a group of reporters who had staked out the Friday meeting. “To be continued. We don’t have any further statements right now. But when we do, we’ll let you know.”

The situation seems pretty simple. Aiyuk arguably owes the 49ers upwards of $13 million. It’s one thing for the 49ers to have the ability to chase him around for the cash; it’s another for the NFL and the 49ers to delay his reinstatement to the league until he wires the funds.

That seems to be precisely where things stand. And the question becomes whether Aiyuk will be willing to strike a deal with the 49ers in order to secure the ability to return to the NFL, with another team.

Instead, a Browns player has provided the next opponent — for the second straight week — with a little extra incentive.

#2839. Seahawks All-22 review: Where the Bears found success against Seattle’s defense

(Editor's note: The following is a guest post by Ryan Woodall, assistant coach at Edmonds-Woodway High School in Edmonds, Washington)

Football and life are games of living and learning, then improving. It's hard to learn when you're dominating. You need a couple punches in the mouth to truly ascend. Think of the Tampa Bay Buccaneers game last year. The Seattle Seahawks allowed 38 points, but after that, the Rams were truly the only team to score 20+ on Seattle. The Cardinals reached 20+ when down 41-15 in the 4th quarter, while the Titans scored a last-second TD to get over 20. This year the Commanders got Seattle early, but the 49ers and Chargers didn't truly feel like their offense dominated.

After watching the film, here are the storylines from Monday's loss to the Chicago Bears:

The Bears stayed committed to running the ball on 1st down. They did so 15 times with only 3 being successful (20%), averaging 1.6 yards on those carries. Overall the Seahawks had the Bears in 2nd & 8+ 12 times Monday. Across the NFL from '22-'26 (regular season), series that have a 2nd & 8+ gain a first down 49% of the time. The Bears gained a 1st down 7/12 for a 58% conversion rate.

Piggybacking off the 2nd & 8+ numbers, the Bears' chances of reaching a 1st down go down significantly with another stop on 2nd down. Across the NFL from '22-'26 (regular season), series with a 3rd & 8+ have a 26% conversion rate, while 3rd & 5+ has a 33% conversion rate.

On 2nd down you don't need it all; you just need to get half back. The Bears had quick route concepts to get what they needed without risking sacks. The quick throws beat some tight coverage. They had runs with combo blocks to move a DL and get up to a backer. Again not playing to get it all, just get to a manageable 3rd. The Seahawks also gave them a few with missed tackles, plus allowed Caleb Williams to escape when the Bears tried to throw deeper.

The Bears know the challenge that comes with facing Seattle. 49% of the time the Seahawks only have 6 defenders in the box. Their light boxes allow the 10th most yards (4.1) while seeing the 6th most rush attempts (52).

The Bears' plan for the light box was to Combo (2 blockers are responsible for 1 DL & 1 LB together) where they could. But you can't combo everyone, especially with only 5 OL, which prompted the Bears to bring in TEs or WRs to help combo with OL or seal off the edge rushers. The combos generally worked, but there's still OL 1v1 blocking, which is where Seattle was able to fight off and help minimize the gains. LBs also did well hitting the OL first, to control the block and ricochet off to make tackles.

November just started, but Caleb Williams wasn't waiting for Thanksgiving to feast. Williams had 17 throws between the numbers and hashes (known as the "seam") for 170 yards, accounting for 69% of his yards with 11 successful. Williams had 7 throws 10+ yards downfield, tallying 99 yards (40% of total) with 5 being successful. On all Dig routes (deep in), Williams was 5/6 for 110 yards (45% of total). In weeks 1-7, the Seahawks allowed 72 yards/game between numbers and hash, with an average target depth (ADOT) of 3.9. This suggests check downs and quick throws were accumulating over the game. Williams finished with 7.6 ADOT.

#2838. Rams listed Matthew Stafford with elbow injury because reporters asked about ice on it

On Monday, reporters asked Rams coach Sean McVay about quarterback Matthew Stafford having ice on his throwing arm after Sunday’s win over the Chargers. On Wednesday, the Rams listed Stafford as having an elbow injury.

“Really, it’s because you guys asked me about it earlier in the week,” McVay told reporters on Friday regarding the decision to disclose an elbow injury for Stafford. “It’s more semantical than anything else. It’s normal, like what he told you guys, but because you guys asked me about him having ice on his elbow. . . . He’s been full, hasn’t missed any sort of reps so normal management stuff that he’s been doing over the last few years.”

It conjures memories of the Patriots’ one-time habit of listing former quarterback Tom Brady as “probable” with a “right shoulder” injury. That all started because he had ice on his shoulder in the locker room, reporters inquired, and the team decided to just put him on the injury report every week.

Has the elbow situation affected Stafford’s throwing?

“I haven’t seen any of that,” McVay said. “I told you guys he’d be better equipped to talk about it. He’s been a full participant, been awesome. He’s spinning the ball. He’s been able to activate all parts of the field with great timing, rhythm, and accuracy so he’s been great.”

So either he has an actual elbow injury that the Rams had been concealing, or he doesn’t really have an elbow injury but they listed him as having one because reporters noticed ice on his arm and asked McVay about it. Neither explanation seems to reflect the true spirit of the injury report, which expects real injuries — and only real injuries — to be disclosed.

Either Stafford has an elbow injury that was previously hidden or he doesn’t have an elbow injury and the claim that he has one is a middle finger to the meddling reporters who were doing their jobs. Neither explanation is a preferred alternative to the unvarnished truth.

#2835. The Protect College Sports Act could be dead

This week’s midterm elections will dramatically reshuffle the deck in Congress. And that could keep those who want the Protect College Sports Act from having the cards necessary to get it passed.

The last, best hope for a Congressional bailout to the college sports system (at the expense of the athletes in the revenue-generating sports) comes in the upcoming six-week lame-duck session. The bill has cleared the Senate. The House of Representatives, which disbanded in the middle of September to block a push for the impeachment of U.S. Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth, has done nothing with it.

The question now becomes whether the short-timers in the House will muster the will to push their own version of the PSCA through to a successful vote.

Senator Ted Cruz (R-Texas), who co-sponsored the original bill, seems to realize that if it doesn’t happen now it never will.

“There is no realistic path to secure the same broad bipartisan support for a new bill in 2027 and beyond,” a spokesperson for the Senate Committee on Commerce, led by Cruz, told The Athletic. “Passing the Senate-approved bipartisan bill through the House and sending it to the President’s desk during the lame duck Congress gives House Republicans an opportunity to close out the 119th Congress with a significant win for President Trump, HBCUs, women’s sports, and student athletes. An attempt to amend it would be a surefire defeat for the colleges and fans counting on PCSA’s enactment.”

At this point, do House Republicans want to give Trump a win? Right or wrong, those who won’t be coming back in January surely believe they lost their bids for re-election because of him. If so, they’ll feel no compulsion to spend time and effort on another presidential pet project at a time when the list of real issues that need to be addressed keeps growing.

If anything, the House would pass an amended version that would require reconciliation while the clock on the 119th Congress ticks toward midnight. The fact that the Senate Commerce Committee regards even an attempt to amend the existing bill as a “surefire defeat” for the bill will make the current House members who soon will be former House members even more likely to say, “Why bother?”

If he pulls it off, Wentz could keep the job for the remainder of the four-game gjallarhorn gauntlet that ends with trips to Wisconsin to play the Packers and Mexico to play the 49ers.

If Wentz fails, the Vikings may mobilize. Immediately.

The Vikings will have roughly 16 hours after Monday night’s game ends to close a deal with another team for a quarterback. On Monday, we made the case for a trade for Colts quarterback Daniel Jones. As the week has unfolded, a variey of indications have emerged that it’s a real possibility.

Jones, who surely is dismayed by the Colts’ decision to prematurely throw in the towel by trading away guard Quenton Nelson and defensive tackle DeForest Bucker, would likely welcome the chance to return to a team with which he spent a couple of months in 2024 — and to lead the effort to propel the neo-Purple People Eaters to the postseason.

The other possibility would be to try to bring Kirk Cousins home. That would be more difficult. At 4-4, the Raiders are on the fringes of contention. Win or lose on Sunday at San Francisco, why disrupt something that’s working? Cousins currently ranks third in passing yards with 2,124 and second in touchdown passes with 19. His passer rating of 101.4 is higher than Josh Allen’s.

Especially since the Colts may already be planning to cut Jones before a $4 million roster bonus comes due in March and $6.51 million of his $33.49 million salary becomes fully-guaranteed.

With a new G.M. coming next year, why wouldn’t the Colts want a clean slate? Doing so would wipe out $38 million in non-guaranteed cash for 2027. Trading Jones would result in getting value; cutting Jones would get them nothing.

It may all come down to Monday night. If Minnesota wins, chances are nothing happens. If Minnesota loses, Tuesday could become a very interesting day.

#2828. What happened to GG Jackson? Why Grizzlies wing was benched vs Thunder

Memphis Grizzlies wing GG Jackson showed signs of a potential breakout season during the preseason.

The 6-foot-9 forward was getting downhill on offense, knocking down 3-pointers and speaking openly about his commitment to becoming a better defender.

After playing regular rotation minutes through the first seven games, Jackson was benched in the second half of the Grizzlies' Nov. 4 loss to the Indiana Pacers. He was replaced by forward Olivier-Maxence Prosper, who Grizzlies coach Tuomas Iisalo said earned more playing time because of his defensive intensity and energy.

It was fair to wonder whether Jackson would get his role back two days later against the Oklahoma City Thunder.

Instead, Grizzlies guard Javon Small entered the rotation, while Jackson did not play in Memphis' 128-97 loss to the Thunder.

Memphis has relied on many of the same rotation players early in the season when they've been available. Jackson is the first player who began the season as a regular contributor to see his role drastically reduced.

"We felt like with Javon Small, having a second ball handler there on the court (and) a second guard, I think those units have been pretty good for us," Iisalo said. "Didn't have a big impact today on the overall score. We saw some burst there, but overall that was the idea behind it."

Jackson is averaging 7.4 points in 15.6 minutes per game. He is shooting 39.6% from the field and 39.1% from 3-point range.

His current box plus-minus, a statistic that estimates a player's impact on team scoring margin per 100 possessions compared with a league-average player, is minus-6.0. That would be a career low for Jackson.

The offensive issues that surfaced against Oklahoma City — Memphis shot 33% from the field and 22.2% from 3-point range — showed that adding another ball handler did not immediately solve the Grizzlies' problems.

Still, Jackson's reduced role appears to be more about the combinations Iisalo wants on the floor than one poor shooting night.

The question now is whether the change is temporary or a sign that Jackson is moving out of the regular rotation.

"It's tough," Grizzlies forward Jerami Grant said. "It's tough anytime your role changes over and over. All we can do is talk to him and try to keep him engaged in the game."

#2826. Recently acquired Seahawks LG Quenton Nelson will need minor knee procedure, is 'week-to-week'

The Seattle Seahawks gave up a pair of draft picks to get six-time All-Pro left guard Quenton Nelson. And just a game into his Seahawks tenure, Nelson — who played a full season in seven of his first eight years with the Indianapolis Colts — is sidelined.

The 30-year-old interior offensive lineman will be out Sunday against the Arizona Cardinals with a knee injury that's requiring a minor procedure, Seattle head coach Mike Macdonald told reporters on Friday.

Nelson is "week-to-week," McDonald said, noting the Seahawks are not going to put him on injured reserve.

"There's a timeline right now that warrants not IR, so that's what we're going to do," Macdonald said.

A battered 4-4 Seattle squad will also be down the likes of quarterback Sam Darnold (quad), left tackle Charles Cross (ankle), wide receiver Rashid Shaheed (knee) and defensive tackle Leonard Williams (stinger) against Arizona. But, following a road game next week versus the Las Vegas Raiders, the Seahawks will have a much-needed bye.

When asked what happened to Nelson, Macdonald said Nelson had something he was dealing with when he arrived from Indianapolis. Given that he passed his physical before the trade went through — from which the Colts received a 2027 third-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick — it's clear that the issue's worsened since.

"We re-imaged it, and it was aggravated to some degree," Macdonald said, "so we've kind of done the due diligence, and everybody agrees it's best to take care of it now and play the long game."

Macdonald said Friday that the Seahawks learned of Nelson's latest development in the last 24 hours.

Nelson, according to NFL Network's Ian Rapoport, will have surgery to clean out some cartilage in his knee. Fragments reportedly have been causing a problem of late.

In Nelson's absence, Anthony Bradford will play right guard, and Grey Zabel — last year's No. 18 overall pick who started at RG during Monday night's loss to the Chicago Bears — will return to left guard, where he played the first seven games.

Drew Lock will get the start at QB this weekend, Macdonald confirmed, even though he's been dealing with a right elbow injury.

Lock stepped in under center earlier this season when Darnold went out with a glute injury in the opener. He guided Seattle to wins over the New England Patriots and the Cardinals, firing four touchdown strikes and no interceptions while completing 72.9% of his passes.

Macdonald said Friday that Seattle managed Lock's throwing this week out of precaution.

Jalen Milroe, whom the Seahawks selected out of Alabama in the third round last year, will back up Lock.

#2825. Holy Cross' Dae'Sean Graves breaks CT career receptions record in win over Woodland

BEACON FALLS - Dae'Sean Graves broke Connecticut's career receptions record Friday night as Holy Cross scored 38 unanswered second-half points to beat Woodland 38-14 in an NVL football game.

Graves entered with 221 career catches, five shy of tying East Catholic's Joey Montalvo's mark of 226. He finished with seven catches for 110 yards and 228 for his career. On Oct. 23, the UConn commit also broke the state career record for touchdown receptions, surpassing Southington's Alex Jamele's mark of 50.

Graves caught a touchdown pass as he broke the state's all-time reception record and led Holy Cross to a 38-14 victory over Woodland #cthsfb #connecticut #highschoolfootball (Kevin Roberts/Hearst Connecticut Media)

The public address announcer noted Graves' achievement Friday, but Graves was already focused on the next play.

"It was definitely good. At the time, I was really focused on the game," Graves said. "That just came, but I wasn't really focused on it. I really was focused on finishing the game. That was my thought process on it, on the announcement of it."

Holy Cross was held scoreless in the first half at Woodland High School, where dense fog made a foghorn an easy substitute for an official's whistle. The Crusaders improved to 7-1 and held onto the No. 2 spot in the Class MM playoff standings behind St. Joseph, which was ranked No. 2 in the latest GametimeCT Top 10 poll. The Hawks fell to 5-3.

That drive took 7:39 off the first-quarter clock. Pringle and Evan Mastropietro took turns on direct snaps from center. Mastropietro also threw a 7-yard touchdown pass to Ryan Kenney in the second quarter.

"We put in a little niche offense, and we're pretty dynamic," Woodland coach Jeremy Clark said. "Our kids are pretty smart, and we had a good game plan for the first half. We executed well, but it was the tale of two halves for us. It really was."

At halftime, Hall told his Holy Cross teammates that they all needed each other.

"I told them we've all got to believe it to achieve it, and that's what we did," Hall said. "We came out there, came out with energy, all believed in each other, had each other's backs, and we did it in the second half."

Holy Cross quickly went to work in the third quarter. Hall had a 30-yard reception on the play before Graves' 32-yard touchdown catch cut the Crusader deficit to 14-7 just 1:02 into the third quarter. After a three-and-out and a short punt, Holy Cross moved the ball 37 yards in a hurry. Graves had a 17-yard catch, then caught a 4-yard pass to break Montalvo's record. Michael Stradford caught an 8-yard touchdown pass to pull Holy Cross within one point, 14-13, with 7:32 left in the third quarter.

On the ensuing Woodland possession, Hall picked off a Mastropietro pass and weaved his way to a 40-yard touchdown and a 20-14 lead. Holy Cross recovered a Pringle fumble on the next Hawk possession, then Lombardo finished the ensuing drive with a 20-yard touchdown pass to Stradford for a 26-14 Crusader lead.

On the first play of the fourth quarter, Hall used an incredible spin move to turn a swing pass from Lombardo into a 69-yard touchdown reception and a 32-14 Holy Cross lead.

"Big players make big plays. That's how I think about it," Graves said. "Aaden made a big move and a big play, got that touchdown. I'm proud of him. He's a good player."

"When you get out-athleted at the end of the day and it's just a better team, and you tip your cap to them," Clark said. "We've got to move forward and have a short memory, because we have two weeks to try to make a push to get into the playoffs."

Lombardo finished the game with four touchdown passes, and he also had several big runs to keep the ball moving for the Crusaders.

Jayden Lewis capped the scoring for Holy Cross later in the fourth quarter with an 11-yard touchdown run.

Dae'Sean Graves, Holy Cross: The senior UConn commit caught seven passes and established himself as Connecticut's new career receptions record holder with 228.

"This is a big win for us. They ended up beating us last year, so this took a big part in our season. We really wanted Woodland this year. That was our mindset this season. We executed our game plan in the second half, and that's how it happened." - Holy Cross' Dae'Sean Graves.

#2824. Watch Luka Doncic's impressive 62-foot 3-pointer to beat halftime buzzer

LOS ANGELES — For as poorly as the Los Angeles Lakers shot the ball in the first half, Luka Dončić's buzzer-beater might serve as a wake-up call.

Dončić got the ball off a Walker Kessler rebound with six seconds left before halftime, dribbled up to the top of the opposite key and launched a 62-foot Hail Mary that he practices religiously. Maybe that's why it looked good coming out of his hand.

The Crypto.com Arena crowd − including Bruce Springsteen − could hardly contain their excitement as the ball sailed through the air, and erupted as it banked in to send the Lakers into the locker room with just their fourth made 3 in 23 attempts, giving them some much-needed momentum as they cut the Portland Trail Blazers' lead to seven points after trailing by as many as 16 in the second quarter.

The Lakers' uncharacteristic offensive struggles on Friday night, however, haven't seemed to rub off on Dončić. He leads the team with 20 points on 43.8% shooting to go along with two assists and two rebounds. He's the only Laker in double figures; Walker Kessler and Austin Reaves are closest to him with eight points apiece.

#2823. Phoenix's David Benavidez ready for 'biggest fight' of boxing career

David Benavidez still frequently runs six miles on South Mountain as part of his training for a fight.

His father, José Benavidez Sr., is now the owner of the gym where his sons learned how to box, Central Boxing Gym near the State Capitol, and even though the family has found a new base of operations in the Seattle-Tacoma area, Phoenix remains close to everyone's heart.

With that in mind, Benavidez planned his next fight, Dec. 5 at Glendale's Desert Diamond Arena, close to his hometown. That's where his two cruiserweight championship belts are on the line against Poland's Michal Cieslak, who holds the WBC cruiserweight title. Benavidez holds the WBA and WBO cruiserweight titles. The December bout would unify all three.

"I'm looking for the hardest fight. If you give me a hard fight, that means a fun night for me," Benavidez said Friday afternoon, Nov. 6, at Vee Quiva Casino, where he'll hold an open workout on Nov. 7 for the public. "I'm a competitor, so whatever he (Cieslak) brings to me, I'm going to be 100 percent ready to deal with it like I deal with it."

Benavidez (32-0) is calling the Dec. 5 fight the biggest of his career, because three titles are on the line. Benavidez has been largely unstoppable over his career, drawing praise from throughout the boxing world for his ability to walk down and punish opponents with precision and fast hands.

The Phoenix area has embraced him, packing arenas when he fights in Arizona and Las Vegas as his career has taken off.

"It's a blessing," Benavidez said. "I'm just very happy and blessed that I can achieve this in my hometown, Phoenix, Arizona. Just being here, it makes me really happy because everything I've wanted or manifested for myself when I was a little kid, when I envisioned becoming a world champion, it's playing in real time now.

"People from Phoenix, we're different. And I'm just happy to continue to show that to the whole world."

Benavidez admitted he never thought he'd become a world champion in three different weight divisions, cruiserweight, light heavyweight and super middleweight. But now he wants to become the best.

"I feel right now that he's the best fighter in the world," Jose Benavidez Sr. said of his 29-year-old son. "I think that he's the face of boxing. ... Sky's the limit. I think he's going to continue winning, looking impressive and keep breaking records.

Another Arizona fighter, Abel Ramos, will fight on Dec. 19 at San Francisco's Chase Center, facing leading contender Brian Norman Jr. in a welterweight bout as part of the Devin Haney-Liam Paro welterweight title main event. Ramos, of Casa Grande, is 29-6-3 but hasn't lost in more than three years.

#2822. Why Bayer Leverkusen's Ibrahim Maza has attracted interest from Arsenal, PSG, Man United and other top clubs

The 2026/27 Bundesliga season is only eight matchdays old, yet there are already some clear early winners. One player who has made another significant leap in his development is Bayer 04 Leverkusen's Ibrahim Maza.

Last season, the midfield talent offered flashes of his ability, though he occasionally struggled with fluctuations in form and had to adapt to unfamiliar roles, such as playing in central midfield. This is hardly surprising, given that it was the Berlin native’s first full season in the Bundesliga.

Maza has since established himself as one of the standout performers of the season so far, delivering impressive displays both as an attacking midfielder and out on the wing.

It is therefore not surprising that the 20-year-old was voted into ninth place in the Young Talent Of The Year election and that there have recently been increasing rumors about him. Clubs including Aston Villa, Arsenal, Manchester United, Tottenham Hotspur, and, most notably, Paris Saint-Germain are reportedly keeping a close eye on him. But what exactly does he bring to the table?

Ibrahim Maza is arguably best suited to the central attacking midfield position. In this role, the Algerian international can dictate the attacking play, set up his teammates in promising positions, and, most importantly, navigate out of tight spaces.

In such situations, he benefits from his significantly improved physical robustness, combined with quick feet and great agility. Paired with his excellent ball control and tricky dribbling, Maza is extremely difficult to dispossess, a fact he has demonstrated impressively on numerous occasions of late.

While Maza can certainly fill in as a wide player or in a deeper central midfield role, his most effective position is as a "number ten," playing just behind Patrik Schick. This is where Maza feels most at home, and he has shown impressive chemistry with the Czech forward this season.

This position also allows him to make the most of his other strengths. Although Maza is quick on his feet and capable of winning duels on the flank thanks to his dribbling ability, he is not the type of "speed-dribbler" seen in players like Lamine Yamal or Yan Diomande. Conversely, a deeper central midfield role doesn't fully play to his strengths, as his focus is primarily on the attacking phase.

In the attacking phase this season, Maza has established himself as both an effective ball-carrier and the creative hub of Leverkusen's attack. Averaging more than three carries per match, he ranks among the best players in his position in the Bundesliga, consistently driving the play forward.

Once in the final third, his remarkable technical ability and high footballing intelligence come to the fore. Maza possesses an intuitive sense for positioning, frequently finding himself in promising scoring positions. It is no coincidence that, among all attacking midfielders, he ranks highly for both shots taken and touches inside the opposition penalty area per game.

Furthermore, from these positions, the German-Algerian is also adept at creating chances for his teammates. More than 1.4 shot assists per game and more than 0.5 smart passes per game are impressive. In addition to his two assists, he also has two pre-assists, which represents an improvement in his goal-scoring threat compared to last season.

Maza is not merely a constant nuisance who poses a direct goal threat; he also consistently demonstrates an eye for his teammates, initiating scoring opportunities and delivering decisive passes. Despite his youth, he has already become a focal point of the Leverkusen attack, regularly driving opposing defenders to distraction with his clever, inventive style of play.

Naturally, there is still room for improvement when it comes to Ibrahim Maza. Aside from his goalscoring output and finishing efficiency, there is little to criticise about his attacking game. He is, after all, a player who can truly make the difference. The situation is different, however, when it comes to his defensive work. Maza could certainly be more tenacious, engage in more duels, and – most importantly – win them with the right level of intensity. Currently, his success rate in ground duels sits at only around 40 percent.

Furthermore, while his creativity and willingness to take risks are assets, he also needs to learn to choose the right moments for such actions. Consequently, there is room for development regarding his decision-making and the number of times he loses possession. At the same time, it is crucial not to stifle his natural joy for the game; the key lies in finding the right balance.

Given his outstanding start to the season, the rumors surrounding this 20-year-old attacking gem come as no surprise. Even during his time at Hertha BSC, Maza was hailed as an exceptional talent; he is simply beginning to realize his full potential more effectively.

Fundamentally, there are several factors Maza should consider when deciding on the next step in his career. To showcase his abilities, he is best suited to a team that prioritizes possession-based football. Furthermore, the club must have a clear role for him, and he needs a realistic prospect of regular playing time. A job-sharing arrangement or a role as a backup is, in all likelihood, not a viable option for him.

Manchester United, meanwhile, may not be the ideal destination for Maza; The Reds are currently grappling with significant issues, and their style of play raises further questions. While Paris Saint-Germain’s footballing philosophy might be a good fit, it is difficult to identify a clear role for him in Luis Enrique's side. There is also speculation that PSG might be looking at Maza as a replacement for Khvicha Kvaratskhelia – who is currently sidelined with a cruciate ligament injury – to play on the wing. Even though a move to PSG might sound enticing, this setup would not be ideal for Maza.

This leaves Arsenal, Aston Villa, and Tottenham Hotspur. However, things are not running entirely smoothly for the team under Roberto De Zerbi (Tottenham), and with the return of Dejan Kulusevski, the presence of James Maddison, and the eventual return of Xavi Simons, there is stiff competition for his position at the club.

The competition is no less intense at Arsenal or Aston Villa. Both clubs possess a wealth of quality in the position Maza plays, meaning the 20-year-old would likely find himself having to wait for his opportunity behind other established players.

Ibrahim Maza undoubtedly has the potential to become a world-class player. If he makes the right decisions about the next steps in his career, he has every chance of reaching that level. In my view, however, none of the clubs currently linked with him offer the right environment; for this reason, I would personally advise against leaving Leverkusen too soon.

At Bayer 04, Maza receives the playing time in his preferred position that he needs for his development. It would therefore be wise for him to remain in Leverkusen at least until next summer before exploring the market for suitable options. A premature departure, by contrast, could, in the worst-case scenario, hinder his development and lead to stagnation.

That said, I believe Maza is capable of holding his own in almost any league. He possesses the necessary technical skills as well as the physical attributes that are particularly in demand in the Premier League. If he fulfils his potential, he could even realise his dream of one day being named on the Ballon d'Or shortlist.

Florian Först is a German scouting expert and the editor-in-chief of Kickfieber.de. He specialises in player analysis and talent scouting, offering insights into the strengths, weaknesses and potential of emerging football talents.

#2821. Shelton Henderson leads No. 14 Miami to 83-57 win over Wagner

CORAL GABLES, Fla. (AP) — Shelton Henderson scored 19 points and No. 14 Miami rebounded from a season-opening loss against top-ranked Florida to beat Wagner 83-57 on Friday night.

Malik Reneau finished with 12 points and Caleb Gaskins added 11 for the Hurricanes (1-1), who outscored Wagner 24-3 in fast-break points and had 18 assists. Miami made 16 of 18 free throws and had a 52-22 advantage in the paint.

Reneau averaged a team-leading 18 points last season while Henderson scored 13 per game but both struggled in the opener Monday against Florida, finishing with five and seven, respectively.

Jones had 13 points and 11 rebounds for the Seahawks (0-2), who feature an entirely new roster under first-year coach Dwan McMillan.

#2818. Player grades: Thunder absolutely humiliate Grizzlies in 128-97 NBA Cup win

Bringing the ball up quickly before the double team could settle, Shai Gilgeous-Alexander contorted himself through traffic. As Jaylen Wells and Jevon Small were sandwiched in between him, the two-time MVP winner went with the circus layup shot attempt that banked in.

The Oklahoma City Thunder absolutely destroyed the Memphis Grizzlies in a 128-97 win. This was also part of the 2026 NBA Cup West Group B play, so yay for bonus points.

If you had thought the Thunder were going to fully lean on Gilgeous-Alexander to bail them out for the next two months, think again. They've regrown an identity that features a much friendlier offensive ecosystem. He took a backseat as Ajay Mitchell and Jared McCain absolutely carved up Memphis at every level.

After snapping an 0-for-18 streak, McCain swished in back-to-back 3-pointers. The Thunder had a 35-28 lead after the first quarter. Everybody chipped in. The momentum continued after the break. OKC built up a 22-9 run in the second frame. The shots were falling. And the Grizzlies looked like a young and inexperienced squad that couldn't handle the consequential setting.

The score started to escalate. Mitchell blew past Ty Jerome for a wide-open layup. That made it a 50-36 game with a little over seven minutes to go in the first half. OKC ballooned its lead with a vintage avalanche run. Mitchell looked like a budding NBA All-Star — completely outplaying the flavor-of-the-week in Jerome.

The Thunder dropped 36 points in the second quarter. They entered halftime with a 71-49 lead over the Grizzlies. Wow. Since the Post-D.C.C. (Dub, Chet, Cason) era started, this was their most stunning showing yet. What a complete 180, where things were about a week ago in their two-game Texas trip.

Any hopes of a miraculous comeback were put to rest. The Thunder dropped the first nine points of the second half. Mitchell had another outside jumper fall on a second-chance look. The energy was off the charts. Even from just watching on the TV screen. The Thunder led by as many as 37 points. That produced some wacky-looking scores as OKC routed one of the hottest teams in the league.

Both teams had 27 points apiece in the third quarter. That made it a 98-76 lead for the Thunder. Just like the good old days, Gilgeous-Alexander enjoyed a short shift. He wasn't required to play the fourth frame. Instead, Mitchell topped his career-best scoring night — which only happened about two weeks ago. He's seamlessly stepped up as the new Robin. OKC had 30 points in the final quarter to fluff up the margin for NBA Cup tiebreaker purposes.

The Thunder shot 55% from the field and went 16-of-35 (45.7%) from 3. They shot 10-of-16 on free throws. They had 25 assists on 51 baskets. Four Thunder players scored double-digit points.

Gilgeous-Alexander finished with 28 points and four assists. Mitchell had a career-best 35 points and five assists. McCain had 21 points and five rebounds. Bennett Stirtz also had 12 points.

Meanwhile, the Grizzlies shot 33% from the field and went 10-of-45 (22.2%) from 3. They shot 29-of-31 on free throws. They had 18 assists on 29 baskets. Five Grizzlies players scored double-digit points.

Jerome had 21 points and five assists. Cedric Coward finished with 10 points and nine rebounds. Zach Edey tallied 14 points and six rebounds. Jerami Grant and Small each scored 10 points apiece.

I mean, what a butt-whooping. The Thunder are back to being a win machine. Sure, they lost some vital limbs — but they aren't dead yet. As long as Gilgeous-Alexander can suit up, they'll always be in any game. Mitchell has completely run away with his newfound opportunity, too. To completely stun the red-hot Grizzlies like this deserves everybody's attention.

Let's see how the Thunder keep things rolling. But Gilgeous-Alexander and Mitchell have proven to be a capable two-man duo since their NBA lives were completely shaken up. And the rest of the supporting cast has stepped up to help OKC not only stay afloat but thrive in its current set of circumstances.

After drawing the shooting foul on Olivier-Maxence Prosper with his pull-up jumper, Gilgeous-Alexander scoped his surroundings. He turned his body in the opposite direction. A group of Memphis kids sitting courtside had their night made as the two-time MVP winner signaled for them to help him get up.

Gilgeous-Alexander finished with 28 points on 11-of-22 shooting, four assists and three rebounds. He shot 3-of-5 from 3 and went 3-of-3 on free throws. He also had one steal and one block.

No Superman heroics were required here. Gilgeous-Alexander enjoyed playing his preferred style of free-flowing basketball. I'm sure he felt a sigh of relief not needing to carry the offense as a one-man show. Instead, he helped bury the Grizzlies on the scoreboard as part of a three-headed scoring machine.

Like in previous years, the Grizzlies had zero answers for Gilgeous-Alexander. He completely dissected their defense. The walking 30-point machine did most of his damage within the perimeter. He buried mid-range jumper after mid-range jumper. When that wasn't there, he ballerina'd his way to the cup. Most of the young Memphis roster learned what one of the greatest scorers ever looks like at the peak of his powers.

Not much mileage was needed out of his heavily-taxed legs. This was a throwback performance to when Gilgeous-Alexander only played in three quarters. He had 11 points in the third frame alone to put this one away. This was especially timely, considering the Thunder are on the front end of a back-to-back.

Add this to the long list of evidence that all the Thunder need is Gilgeous-Alexander. As long as he's on the court, they're a regular-season high-win machine. Been easy to scoff at that theory in recent years due to their All-NBA talent elsewhere, but there's no denying it right now with how bad the health situation is. That's why I think OKC should ultimately be OK for the rest of the regular season. Even with their absences. He's just too dang good not to be a competitive contender.

Absolutely bullying his way around Grant, Mitchell went with the crossover to go left. As he went with the awkwardly-angled driving layup, the 24-year-old capped off his career night with a blue-collar and-one bucket. It showed off both his finesse and raw strength.

Mitchell finished with 35 points on 14-of-20 shooting, five assists and one rebound. He shot 4-of-7 from 3 and went 3-of-5 on free throws.

Not to get too ahead of things, but if the Thunder hope to continue their streak of two NBA All-Star players, I think we've found the best candidate. Not even two weeks ago, Mitchell had his first 30-ball of his career. Now, he's easily topped that again here. He completely took apart Memphis' defense and added to OKC's historic one-sided competitiveness with the Grizzlies.

It was two spots that Mitchell completely dominated on the court — in the paint and outside the perimeter. The former shouldn't be a surprise. His drive-heavy scoring attack has turned him from a second-round two-way to Gilgeous-Alexander's Dick Grayson. But the outside jumper? That has finally turned a corner after an icy start to the year.

As the Thunder put up some hilarious scoreboard numbers, Mitchell had 21 points in the first half. Nobody on the Grizzlies could stay in front of him. Poor Jerome had his subpar defense exposed at the NBA Cup level. For good measure, he added 14 points to his total in a drama-free second half that should've been played on a running clock. Not only did he cross 30 points again, but he flirted with 40 in his best scoring output yet.

Mitchell was in the zone. All night. He has the right combination of strength and finesse. He powered through several smaller defenders to get to the rim. When that didn't work, he tried his luck with a bunch of circus shots that feathered through. The outside jumpers were just straight-up insulting. It's been a minute since OKC completely dominated another team like this from tip-off to the final buzzer, but it's found its footing with this new group.

Since Mitchell was jettisoned from Sixth Man to second go-to scorer, he's stepped up to the plate. He's turned into a 20-point scorer. With very few lulls. Consistency is what separates a high-end role player from a certified top star. Right now, we're seeing him take that next step in his improbable developmental arc.

On a pair of catch-and-shoot looks, McCain rediscovered his groove. The Thunder quickly put up a double-digit lead. They can thank their en-fuego-style outside shooting for that. After a cold spell, the 22-year-old showed what the positive side of his shooting variance can look like.

McCain finished with 21 points on 9-of-14 shooting and five rebounds. He shot 3-of-8 from 3. He also had four steals and one block.

Do you think McCain heard Stirtz's footsteps coming for his playing time? He's certainly played like somebody with a fire lit underneath their seat. Showing an inkling of confidence, the microwave scorer was hot throughout the night. Sure, the outside jumpers fell. But his scoring attack within the paint has also been a nice surprise so far.

McCain had 12 points in the first quarter. He helped the Thunder get away from this one early. As Gilgeous-Alexander gladly took a backseat after back-to-back 40-point nights, it was he and Mitchell who delivered OKC's first lethal punch to Memphis. The two absolutely demoralized the hot-shot, young, up-and-coming squad.

This is what the best version of McCain looks like. When he's zoned in, he needs little time to drop points in a flurry. The Thunder have learned to play a team-friendly offensive style that lessens the burden on Gilgeous-Alexander. This is what it tangibly looks like. He made OKC's decision to start him look like an absolute genius move.

Fighting on the glass with Cameron Boozer and Coward, Mara came up with the win on the loose ball. He tapped the ball towards him. He muscled his way up for the layup attempt. It didn't go in, but he drew the shooting foul. Finally, he thought. He celebrated the call and screamed at the referees' direction.

Mara finished with nine points on 4-of-6 shooting and seven rebounds. He shot 1-of-4 on free throws. He also had one block.

It only took a little past Halloween for the Thunder to learn how to utilize Mara. They threw the ball up with the mindset that he'd Prime Megatron would catch it. In their defense, the plan has worked without a hitch. He's turned into their new favorite toy as an automatic assist connection. The lob threat is an improved wrinkle in OKC's offense. Something that has seldom been there over the years.

Even coming off the bench, Mara completely outplayed both of the Grizzlies' dynamic centers in Boozer and Edey. On defense, he had a nasty recovery block that showed his defensive gravity. The 21-year-old has learned on the job. Playing a larger role than expected, he's helped the Thunder fortify a frontcourt rotation with all of the injuries they've experienced.

Couldn't have dreamt up a better start to Mara's NBA journey. He's helped the Thunder get real wins already. Both as a starter and bench player. It's been a couple of years since OKC has had fresh-faced rookies help them from the jump. Now, it's getting a double dose of that with the seven-footer and Stirtz.

This article originally appeared on OKC Thunder Wire: Player grades: Thunder absolutely humiliate Grizzlies in 128-97 NBA Cup win
